## 2026-09-23 (audit follow-up, Batch A: the corpus said pass where it should say fail)

**D-111 (2026-09-23) — Batch A is a patch release (0.2.1 when tagged), not a major one; and A1: the TLE epoch field rendered an exact midnight as `YYDDD.-8000000`, fixed with a regression test.** Versioning (owner asked for the agent's reading of the README rule): the rule makes "changed `expected.json` schema or check semantics" the major trigger, and check semantics means what a conforming parser must do. None of the five Batch A items changes that: a parser that parses every record, matches every id and reads unchanged input passes exactly as before; the items only stop crediting outcomes that were never meant to count as passes (zero records read, an unmatched id in `check-tle`, drifted stable input treated as live) and fix a renderer defect and a metadata error. No expected value, check text or schema changes; A4's tier correction touches provenance metadata, not a frozen value, so the freeze rule holds. The visible consequence, a run that passed vacuously and now fails, is what a fix looks like, and the changelog's `[Unreleased]` section lists the five under "Fixed". A1, the defect: `epoch_field` (`gpconf/tle.py`) built the fraction with `str(frac)[2:]`, and `Decimal(0).quantize(Decimal('0.00000001'))` prints as `0E-8`, so any exact-midnight epoch, including the carry from 23:59:59.999999 under half-up rounding, rendered as `-8000000`: a 69-character line with a valid checksum (the minus counts 1) that the reference reader rejects, and through `writer.round_trip` a correct writer was failed at every midnight epoch. Latent in the frozen data because no input lies within 4.32 ms of midnight, which is why the 304/304 render validation and the 1,208-record round trip never reached the branch. Fix: `format(frac, 'f')[2:]`. Tests (`tests/test_roundtrip.py`, `EpochFieldTests`, failing before the fix and passing after): exact midnight, one second after midnight (`.00001157`), the last microsecond of the day under half-up (carries to the next day) and truncation (`.99999999`), the year rollover, and a rendered midnight line read back by the reference reader. Note on inputs: the ISO epoch is parsed at microsecond resolution, so an eight-decimal second such as 23:59:59.99999999 is not an accepted input; the TLE field's 864 µs resolution makes microseconds sufficient. Checked for this entry: the four failing assertions before the fix, the five passing after, the reference run 17/17 exact, and the writer judge at a midnight epoch reporting no mismatch.

**D-112 (2026-09-23) — A2: `check-tle --against` fails a written record whose catalog number has no match in the source records; it used to pass with a note.** The audit's reproduction: `check-tle fixtures/analyst-objects/raw/analyst-81011-first.tle --against fixtures/six-digit-omm-saramago/raw/saramago-first.csv` printed `[pass]`, the note "no source record with id 81011 in the --against file; round trip not checked", and exited 0. On the satno2tle path that is the likely failure mode itself: `auto.sh` passes the catalog field's text to `rffit -i`, an Alpha-5 field becomes 0 through `atoi`, rffit prints "No elements found" and continues with its zero-initialised orbit (rffit.c line 40; D-097, reading complete, not run), and the written line carries `00000`; a user who then checked the file against the source records got a pass for a TLE about no object. Now (`gpconf/writer.py`, `check_record`): an unmatched id is a problem, so the record fails and the command exits 1; the detail says "no source record with id N in the --against file; the round trip cannot be checked", and for N = 0 adds "likely cause: 00000 is what rffit writes when its -i lookup found no elements and the orbit stayed zero-initialised, e.g. an Alpha-5 field passed through satno2tle", for N = 99999 "likely cause: 99999 is rffit's default catalog number". Both causes are stated as likely, since the rffit behaviour is established by reading its source, not by a run. Tests (`tests/test_writer.py`, `CheckTleTests`): a file of four rendered records (100000, 123456, 00000, 99999) checked against a source file holding only 100000 exits 1 with the matched record passing, the other three failing, the rffit cause named only for 0 and 99999; a file of matched records only still exits 0; the older test that asserted the note-and-pass behaviour now asserts the failure. Both were failing before the change (one error, one assertion) and pass after; the full suite passes (43 tests). `docs/WRITERS.md` says so. Checked for this entry: the reproduction's exit code measured directly (1 after the change) and the reference run, unchanged at 17 of 17.

**D-113 (2026-09-23) — A3: a source file the reference reader reads zero records from, where the manifest records some, now fails the case with a description of the file; it used to pass every per-file check on zero records.** The audit reproduced it on a scratch copy: `iss.tle` replaced by an HTML 403 body, a TLE cut off after line 1, an empty KVN variant and a BOM-prefixed one all left the case at `pass`, with `tle-checksums-valid` reporting "0 record(s), all checksums valid" and the derived-KVN checks "variant parsed", because the values item is only emitted when an oracle exists and the remaining checks count zero records as success. That is the state `tools/fetch.py` leaves behind after an unexpected HTTP response, so a user who hit a rate-limit page got a green run. Rule now (`gpconf/runner.py`, `load_source`, before the parser under test is called): if the source's recorded `record_count` is positive and the reference reader returns no records, the item `source-readable` fails with "reference reader found 0 of N expected records; the file is <size> bytes, starts with <first 48 bytes>; looks like: <guess>", the guess being any of: empty file, whitespace only, UTF-8 BOM prefix, HTML document, CelesTrak's empty-response text, a TLE line 1 without a line 2, CSV header only (`describe_bytes`); the source's state becomes `unreadable`, which every per-kind loop already treats like a parse error, so no vacuous check runs for that file. A second rule covers the reader itself: when the file's hash matches the tested snapshot and the reader's count differs from the recorded one, the same item fails as an internal inconsistency. Live-mode files (hash differs) with a non-zero count are not count-checked, since rolling windows change their record counts legitimately. Distinguished on purpose: the 16-byte "No GP data found" bodies of the 404 sources carry `record_count` 0 and `http_status` 404 and keep their `empty-404` state; a positive `record_count` is the trigger, never the count zero. Tests (`tests/test_source_reading.py`, failing before and passing after): the four reproductions through `load_source`, each asserting the state, the item, the size, the quoted first bytes and the guess words; the 404 body staying `empty-404` with no failure; a readable file staying `ok` and a live file with a different count staying `ok`; a snapshot file with a wrong recorded count failing as internal; and, through `Runner.run` on a scratch root holding the shipped `kvn-syntax-variants` case with its first variant emptied, the case status `fail` with exactly one `source-readable` item, no "variant parsed" item for that file and the other variants still passing. Checked for this entry: the full suite (48), the reference run 17 of 17 exact, and the naive and python-sgp4 runs unchanged at 14 and 7 failing cases, so no legitimately passing file changed status. The README describes the item where it explains the runner's statuses.

**D-114 (2026-09-23) — A4: the writer case's source files carry the tier the case that owns each file recorded, not a blanket "stable"; the two rolling group files are live again, and `tools/fetch.py --check-drift` counts 22 stable sources, not 24.** `build_writer` (`tools/make_expected.py`) listed every file its inputs came from with `source_entry(path, "stable", ...)`, because the inputs themselves are frozen records; but the files include `fixtures/analyst-objects/raw/analyst.csv` and `fixtures/tle-omits-six-digit-objects/raw/last-30-days.csv`, the group snapshots the derived Alpha-5 lines were rendered from, which their own cases record as live. `manifest_sources` in `tools/fetch.py` takes "stable" for a path if any case says so, so those two rolling queries became stable-tier in the manifest: the audit's `--check-drift` counted 24 stable sources where the corpus has 22, and the first user to run the fetch tool after those groups update (every two hours) would have been told two stable sources drifted. The "stable if any case says so" rule stays: it is right when no case mislabels a file, and the fix is at the label. Now `originating_tier(path, from_case)` returns the tier recorded for the path by the case the input came from, or, when that case does not list the file (a derived line's canonical source belongs to another case), by the case that owns the fixture folder; a path no case records is labelled live and reported as a PROBLEM. The frozen input records keep tier stable, as before: the records are frozen values, the files are not. Regenerated: `fixtures/tle-writer-alpha5/expected.json` (the two tiers and the generation stamp change, nothing else), `manifest.json`, `MANIFEST.md` and the case document. Tests: `ExpectedInputsTests.test_sources_keep_their_originating_tier` checks every writer source against the tiers the other cases record for the same path, excluding the case-level label "mixed", and pins the two group files as live and the records as stable; `RealManifestTiers` in `tests/test_fetch_drift.py` checks `manifest_sources` on the real manifest: the two files live, and 22 stable raw fixture files, the count the tracker site checks. Both fail against the pre-fix data (a `stable != live` assertion; 36 stable paths counted before the fixture-only restriction, since the shipped derived and vector files are stable too) and pass after. Checked for this entry: `--check-drift` on disk (61 files match, 22 stable, 39 live, no drift), the full suite (50), the reference run 17 of 17 exact, and the expected.json diff limited to the two tiers and the stamp.


**D-115 (2026-09-23) — A5: a stable-tier source whose bytes differ from the recorded SHA-256 is reported as drift, explicitly: a failing `stable-source-drift` item in the case output, a `drift` field in the JSON report, and a values item that says the frozen expected values were not applied and why.** Before, `load_source` set the mode to "live" for any hash mismatch regardless of tier, the reference reader silently became the oracle, and the only trace was an `info` item shown with `--verbose`; the audit reproduced it by changing one digit of a stable file and reading `[pass] values` in the table. The tier promise is that a stable source is frozen (README, Versioning), so changed bytes mean either that the provider altered a first-ever record, which the maintainer must learn, or that the user's file is corrupt; either way the case as published could not be executed, and a green table would hide it. Status `fail` for the drift item, deliberately: the case's status then says the run could not deliver what the case promises, while the parser's own verdict stays visible in the values item, which is still computed against the reference reader and labelled "frozen expected values not applied: the stable source drifted from the tested snapshot". Live-tier sources are unchanged: rolling windows are expected to differ and keep the "live bytes" label. The run footer lists every drifted file and points at `tools/fetch.py --check-drift`; `CaseResult.as_dict()` carries `drift` as path -> recorded and actual SHA-256, so the JSON report and `docs/FAILURES.md`, which is generated from it, would show a drift if one ever occurred. Tests (`tests/test_stable_drift.py`, on a scratch root holding the shipped `alpha5-tle-derived` case, whose four sources are stable-tier): an untouched copy passes with all four values items "match frozen expected values" and an empty `drift`; a copy with one mean-motion digit changed and the checksum recomputed fails with exactly one failing item, `stable-source-drift` on that file, its detail naming the tier, the mismatch, both hashes and the reference comparison, a `drift` entry with a 64-character actual hash different from the recorded one, that file's values item passing (the reference adapter agrees with the reference reader) with the not-applied label, and the other three files still exact against the frozen values. Both tests fail before the change (a missing `drift` key; status pass) and pass after. Checked for this entry: the full suite (52), the reference run 17 of 17 exact on the untouched corpus, and the on-disk drift check (0 stable drift), so no item appears on a clean checkout.

**D-116 (2026-09-23) — Batch C, corpus: a stale count, two footnotes and the dating of entries.** The ambiguity `mmdot-convention` in `tools/cases.py` said "(301/301 records)", the Phase 2 count, while the case and the README count 304 of 304 pairs; it now says 304/304, and `MANIFEST.md` and the five case documents that copy the text were regenerated. `MANIFEST.md`'s summary table now carries a footnote that its records column sums the record counts of a case's source files, since `analyst-objects` shows 567 (565 group records plus the two single-object first fetches) against the 565 the text elsewhere quotes. The 0.2.0 changelog entry keeps "drafted and not sent" as the state at release and gains a bracketed note that the strf note was filed after the release as cbassa/strf#88 (D-108), so the two statements no longer read as a contradiction. Dating: entries D-100 to D-109 carried no date and D-104 to D-110 sat under a 2026-09-22 heading although they record events of the 23rd (UTC dates of the commits that introduced them: D-100 to D-103 on 2026-09-22, D-104 onward on 2026-09-23); each now carries its date after its number in the form D-111 introduced, a "2026-09-23" heading precedes D-104, and the preamble says every entry from D-100 on carries its date. The corpus handoff, whose filename carries the date it was first written, says at the top that it is the living handoff. No expected value, check text or schema changes. Checked for this entry: the regenerated manifest and case documents contain no "301/301"; the full suite; the export verified from a fresh copy.

## 2026-09-23 (audit follow-up, Batch D: runner and reader robustness)

**D-117 (2026-09-23) — D1: the reference readers reject malformed input with a clear error instead of returning a wrong record; `from_alpha5` follows Space-Track's definition strictly.** Audit item 10.9 found the readers silent on five inputs: a UTF-8 BOM (the CSV lost its first column name, the KVN read zero messages, a 2LE lost its first record), an XML document with a default namespace (one record with every field None, because the `find` paths are not namespace-qualified), a repeated keyword in one KVN message (last value won), a TLE epoch day of 000 or 367 (landed in the previous or next year), and `from_alpha5` accepting `'A-001'`, `'A 000'` and `'+1234'` through `strip` and `int`. Each is now a `ValueError` naming the problem: `read_file` refuses a file that starts with a BOM, since the provider formats carry none; `read_xml_text` refuses a root element in a namespace, since the corpus reads the unqualified NDM/XML form only; `read_kvn_text` refuses a keyword repeated within a message; `tle_epoch_to_iso` requires the day of year to lie in 001..365, or 366 in a leap year; `from_alpha5` requires exactly five characters, a digit or an uppercase letter other than I and O first and four digits after, which is Space-Track's definition and what `writer.strict_catalog_field` and the reference adapter's hook already enforced around it. In the runner, `load_source` reports a `ValueError` from the reader as "reference reader rejected the file: …" rather than as an internal error; other exceptions keep the internal label. Checked before writing the fix: a scan of all 84 raw and derived files found none with a BOM, none with an XML default namespace, no repeated KVN keyword, no TLE day outside its year, and every catalog field already in the strict form, so no passing case changes; and the adapter counts before (naive 14 failing cases with 76 items, python-sgp4 7 with 20) equal the counts after, because the naive adapter uses `int()` and the python-sgp4 adapter uses that library's own decoder. Tests: `tests/test_malformed_input.py` pins each of the five behaviours, failing before the change (fifteen sub-failures) and passing after; the A3 test for a BOM-prefixed KVN file now expects the reader's rejection, the clearer of the two errors. Suite and reference run checked with exit codes.

**D-118 (2026-09-23) — D2, runner completeness: the three listed-but-unevaluated checks are evaluated; `--vectors-cmd` reaches `parse_catalog_id`; a raising hook fails its vector, not the case; `norm_epoch` accepts UTC offsets and a leap second; the JSON report names a `--write-cmd` parser; the failure catalogue withholds SupGP values for every value-carrying check; `ndot_field` rounds half up.** 10.8, implemented rather than removed: `leading-dot-decimals`, `bstar-implied-decimal-exponent` and `negative-bstar-and-ndot` were in the manifest and three case documents but emitted no item, so a reader saw them "pass" by omission; the case documents present them as distinct checks and each is a few lines, so `Runner.check_field_forms` now compares the fields each check names against the same oracle the values item uses (frozen values in snapshot mode, the reference reader otherwise): the OMM keywords written without a leading zero in the file's own text (found by `leading_dot_keywords`: `.00048259`, `-.15975118E-3`), the BSTAR and second-derivative fields of every TLE record, and the BSTAR and first-derivative fields of the records whose oracle value is negative. A file without the feature emits nothing, and a case with none at all reports `not-exercised`, like the nine-digit check. A sub-tolerance agreement counts as a pass with the count in the detail; the values item already carries the tolerance report. Counts before and after: naive 14 failing cases with 76 items, python-sgp4 7 with 20, unchanged, because both adapters' values already agreed on those fields where the new items apply and the naive failures in `bstar-and-derivative-forms` are parse failures on a different file. 10.11: `parse_catalog_id` joins the four ops the external command protocol dispatches, so `--vectors-cmd` parsers get the nine-digit text vectors instead of a skip; a command answer without `result` or `error`, or that is not JSON, is a `ValueError` naming it rather than a `KeyError`. 10.10: the `two_digit_year` loop calls the hook once per vector inside a try, so a raising hook fails that vector with the exception's name and the other items are still produced; before, the whole vectors case ended as "internal error in runner". 10.12: `norm_epoch` accepts a `[+-]HH:MM` offset (converted to UTC) and `:60` (mapped to `:59`, as `tests/adapters/reference.py` already does in its own epoch parsing), so a parser returning an aware `isoformat()` or the CCSDS leap-second form is no longer reported as "parser raised". 10.15: the JSON report's `parser` names the `--write-cmd` command; `tools/make_failures.py` gains `render(reps, generated_at)` for testing and withholds failing details for the checks that embed element values (`values`, `omm-formats-agree`, `tle-values-match-omm-within-tle-precision`, `mmdot-is-tle-field-value`, `tle-writer-round-trip` and the three above) on SupGP-sourced files, while a structural detail such as a parse exception stays readable; `ndot_field` quantises half up like every other rendered field (`0.000000125` → `.00000013`, was `.00000012` under the Decimal context's half-even), scanned across every record the renderer touches, the 1,208 records of the round-trip test (604 from the CelesTrak TLE/2LE files on disk, 604 derived Alpha-5 lines) and the 609 records of the writer case: no MEAN_MOTION_DOT value carries more than eight decimals (1,651 have exactly eight, 148 seven, 14 six, 4 none), so there are 0 exact ties at the eighth decimal and 0 lines render differently under half-up than under half-even. The change is unobservable in current inputs. It rests on the rendering rule the corpus established empirically for the BSTAR and second-derivative mantissas (D-028, D-071: 304 CelesTrak record pairs) and on consistency with every other rendered field, which already quantises half up; whether the provider itself rounds a tie half up is not backed by a provider document and is the coverage gap `tle-vs-omm-precision-loss` and the writer case already record ("no rounding tie … was observed") [untested]. CLAUDE.md gains the zsh note beside the exit-code rule. Tests: `tests/test_runner_completeness.py`, nine tests failing before and passing after, including a general one that every check a case lists appears among its items for the reference adapter; the listed-checks tests skip in the public clone, where the raw fixtures are absent. Suite and the three adapter runs checked with exit codes.

**D-119 (2026-09-23) — D3, check-tle and writer robustness: check-tle reports unpaired, indented and BOM-prefixed element lines instead of dropping them; a day-of-year source epoch round-trips; every written record is checked for column layout; the epoch-string vectors compare the instant; `tools/fetch.py` never re-requests a file that lacks its metadata, requests a re-capture only after its original and never a URL twice in a run, judges the two-hour rule by `retrieved_at`, and exits 2 on stable drift; negative tests for wrong values, tolerance boundaries and the command protocol.** 10.6: `extract_records` paired only `1 `/`2 ` lines that began at column 1, so an element line without its partner, an indented pair and a byte-order mark vanished and the file exited 0, or 1 as "nothing to check" without saying why. `writer.scan_lines` now returns the records (with the indentation and the BOM as problems on the record, the lines checked with the indentation removed) and the strays; `check_file` emits one failing entry per stray (`unpaired: true`) and the CLI prints and counts them. 10.7: `writer._iso` normalised nothing, so a `--against` record whose EPOCH was the day-of-year form (legal in CCSDS 7.5.10; the corpus's own KVN case declares it) raised an uncaught `ValueError` out of `tle.epoch_field`. The normaliser `tle.iso_epoch` (calendar and day-of-year forms, any fraction length quantised to microseconds, an optional Z, a `+HH:MM`/`-HH:MM` offset, second 60) now serves `epoch_field`, `runner.norm_epoch` (which delegates to it; D-118's inline code is replaced, same behaviour, same tests) and `writer._iso`; the round trip in `check_record` is wrapped so a source value the corpus cannot read is a problem on the record, not a crash. It lives in `gpconf/tle.py` because the reader imports the renderer and the runner imports both. 10.4: `writer.check_layout` pins every field of both lines to its columns (the decimal points at columns 24 and 35 of line 1 and 12, 21, 38, 47 and 55 of line 2, the separators, digits-only and right-justified fields); it runs in `check_record` and as the new writer item `tle-writer-column-layout`, added to `CHECKS` and to the case (expected.json, case.md, manifest and MANIFEST.md regenerated offline). The gap was real: the test builds a line 2 with a left-justified inclination and a recomputed checksum, and `check_lines`, `check_catalog_field` and `round_trip` all pass on it. Every provider and derived record the round-trip test renders (1,208) and python-sgp4's 606 exports have the standard layout. 10.5: `vectors/ccsds-epoch-strings.json` carries `iso`, the instant each valid string denotes (values computed by the reference adapter and checked against the calendar by hand: day 64 of 2020 is 4 March, day 204 of 2002 is 23 July), plus `iso_alternatives` for the leap second (23:59:59 or the next midnight, since a datetime cannot carry second 60); the runner compares the hook's result through `norm_epoch` within the 2 µs tolerance, so a hook returning a constant fails and names the vector. `ccsds-epoch-strings` joins `CHECKS` and the vectors case's list: the runner had emitted it since v0.1.0 but no manifest or case document described it. Counts before and after, over all cases: naive 14 failing cases with 76 items → 14 with 76; python-sgp4 7 with 20 → 7 with 20; reference 17 exact → 17 exact. The layout item passes for all three writers (naive's six-digit lines are 70 characters and fail the length check before any layout check; its three five-digit records are aligned); the epoch item is unchanged for naive (it already failed, rejecting five valid forms) and python-sgp4's adapter exposes no `parse_epoch`, so it still skips. 10.14: `fetch.py` gains `plan()` (what a run would do per entry, without the network), `run()` (the command line, parameterised by root, entries, clock and fetch function; `main` is `sys.exit(run())`), `duplicate_urls`, `read_meta` and `age_seconds`. A raw file on disk counts as fetched whether or not its metadata exists (before, a missing `.meta.json` caused a fresh request); `--force` reads the recorded `retrieved_at` and falls back to mtime only without metadata (a checkout or a sync rewrites mtimes); a re-capture entry is requested only with `--include-recaptures`, when its original is on disk, was retrieved at least two hours earlier and is not itself requested in the run (before, a fresh checkout with `--include-recaptures` fetched the same endpoint twice two seconds apart); no URL is requested twice in a run; the exit code is 2 when a stable-tier source drifted. Thirteen tests drive `plan` and `run` with a stub in place of `fetch_one` and a temporary root; no request is made. README and the script's docstring updated. 10.16: `tests/test_negative.py` shows a changed mean motion, catalog number and epoch are detected and named (an epoch off by 3 µs fails, by 2 µs passes within tolerance), pins the boundaries (2 µs inclusive, 432 µs for TLE epochs, 1e-12 relative for float-derived values, zero requires zero, exact decimals get no tolerance) and drives `CommandParser.parse` through `{fmt}` substitution, exit 3, a non-zero exit (the exit code and stderr are named), a JSON object instead of an array and output that is not JSON (now a `RuntimeError` naming it; before, a bare `JSONDecodeError`). The audit's last 10.16 sub-item, the naive must-fail test that passed vacuously in the public clone for the three cases whose CelesTrak files are absent (`six-digit-omm-saramago`, `analyst-objects`, `supgp-celestrak-classification-c`): the test is split. The four cases that ship with the repository are asserted unconditionally; for the three fetched-source cases the test asserts the failure where the files are present, asserts that a case without its files consists of `source-present` skips only (never a pass), and then reports itself skipped with the reason and the list of cases, so a reader of a public-clone run sees why. The alternative, driving the three cases from the frozen records, was not taken: the naive parser's failures there are parse failures on the provider's own bytes (six-digit ids, an empty OBJECT_ID, classification C), so rendering the frozen records back into CSV, JSON, XML, KVN and TLE would test the corpus's rendering rather than the provider's files, and the SupGP case's values are withheld from the export (D-049), so it could not be reconstructed at all; the decision policy prefers omitting to fabricating. Found while verifying the export as a fresh tree: a case none of whose files is on disk reported "not exercised" rather than "skip" when it listed a check with a case-level fallback item (`nine-digit-ids-parse` since v0.1.0 for `nine-digit-supgp-launch-nominals`; the three field-form checks of D-118 for `epoch-year-19xx`, `baseline-iss-five-formats` and `bstar-and-derivative-forms`), and the item's detail claimed the fetched data lacked the feature when nothing had been fetched. The fallbacks now fire only after at least one source was read; `tests/test_runner.py` `SourcelessCases` runs seven cases from a root holding only `manifest.json` and their `expected.json` and asserts every one is skipped with `source-present` items only (failing before the guard: `'not-exercised' != 'skip'`). In this checkout, where every source is present, the adapter counts do not change. Tests: `tests/test_check_tle_robustness.py` (16), `tests/test_fetch_requests.py` (13), `tests/test_negative.py` (19), all failing before the changes except where they pin existing behaviour; suite 114 under both Pythons, the three adapter runs and the catalogue regeneration checked with exit codes.

**D-120 (2026-09-23) — Release 0.2.1 prepared (owner instruction), not committed, tagged or pushed: version constants bumped from 0.2.0 to 0.2.1 (`tools/make_manifest.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `gpconf/__init__.py`, README, `CITATION.cff`), the changelog heading set to 0.2.1 with today's date, manifest and failure catalogue regenerated from fresh runs of the three adapters, export regenerated into a scratch tree and verified there with exit codes checked (suite under both Pythons, reference run).** Same sequence as D-102. The audit statement in the README status paragraph, the README's audit bullet, the changelog and `CITATION.cff` says the independent audit covered v0.1.0 and that neither the writer-side case of v0.2.0 nor the Batch A–D fixes of v0.2.1 were separately audited; the audit of 2026-09-23 that produced the fixes is named as a read-only pass by a separate AI session under the maintainer's direction, not an independent audit. The version DOI slot is left as at D-102, a comment in `CITATION.cff` and a sentence in the README and the changelog; Zenodo mints it at the release, and D-106's sequence then fills it. Classification: D-111 decided patch for Batch A; Batch D added one check to the writer case and documented one long-evaluated check, which makes the corpus stricter without changing a frozen value or the schema, and the owner directed 0.2.1; the changelog paragraph names the added and the documented check rather than claiming no check text changed. `date-released` is today, the date of the tagged content. GitHub release notes drafted from the changelog in the shape of v0.2.0's (kept outside the repository, as v0.2.0's were; the published text is the record). The public site still describes v0.2.0 and is updated after the tag, as for 0.2.0.

**D-121 (2026-09-23) — GitHub release v0.2.1 published at the owner's request ("Publish it."), from the app's built-in browser in the owner's own GitHub session: title "v0.2.1 — audit follow-up fixes", existing tag v0.2.1 at b1407a5, label Latest, not a pre-release, published 2026-09-23T21:09:22Z.** The body is `docs/handoff/release-notes-v0.2.1-draft.md` from its third line on (the first line is the title itself and would have rendered as a stray line; the owner saw this before publishing), with the five "How to run" commands wrapped in a `bash` fence at the owner's request so they render as one code block; the SHA-256 of the form's body matched the file's before publishing, so the published text and the private draft are identical. The draft's lead names eight decision entries (D-111 to D-115, D-117 to D-119); the owner's proposed "eleven fixes across five decision entries" could not be reconciled with the changelog and was flagged before the owner chose to publish the eight. The form was filled and the Preview checked before the owner said the word; the pane was hidden, so the fill, the tab switches and the Publish click were script-driven and each state was read back from the DOM. Zenodo's version DOI for v0.2.1 is pending at the time of this entry (the concept record 22867654 lists v0.1.0 and v0.2.0 only); when it appears the D-106 sequence fills the three marked slots, and the export and push wait for the owner's word.

**D-122 (2026-09-23) — Batch E1, corpus documentation read as a stranger (audit section 5, every item).** README: the quick-start comment on the control run says the nine-digit case reports not-exercised outside a launch window; "18 SDS" is expanded to the 18th Space Defense Squadron at first use (README, the nine-digit case title, the SupGP interpretation); a third tier bullet defines `mixed` (the pairs, facts and XML-schema cases list files fetched for other cases, use them structurally, and drift is judged by the fetching case's tier, D-114) and says vector and derived files are never fetched; the adapter section counts five vector hooks and the example gains `parse_catalog_id`. Case documents (generated by `tools/make_manifest.py`): every case with `raw/` sources opens its Sources section with one sentence saying `tools/fetch.py` creates those files and the runner skips until then, and a case whose files all ship says so; the source table prints a real status and time for every row: an expected 404 names its body ("No GP data found", "No SupGP data found"), a derived file says it is shipped and when it was generated (from its provenance sidecar), a vector file says it is shipped and never fetched; a re-capture row carries a note naming its original and D-022 and stating that the `FORMAT` value is spelled in lower case (the reason for the spelling is not recorded; the entries are distinct through `recapture_of`, not through the spelling, since the duplicate guard is case-insensitive). The nine-digit case's gap text no longer quotes a status the runner never emits; it says `nine-digit-ids-parse` reports `not-exercised`. "hand-recorded" in the six-digit-TLE case is explained (a tool defect at 00:10Z discarded the body, reconstructed by hand from the tool's log, D-013; clean re-capture at 00:41Z, D-022). The vectors case's title and coverage now name all four vector sets. The check text of `catalog-number-is-integer` says a ten-digit value is rejected, which the vector file already tested. `docs/FAILURES.md` (generated by `tools/make_failures.py`): each case entry opens with its failing-item count and says how many items were collapsed into a shown bullet, and a collapsed bullet names the files it stands for, so the table's count and the bullets reconcile; a detail cut at 600 characters ends in an ellipsis and the reading guide says the JSON report holds the full text; a withheld SupGP detail says how to see it (run the case on your own fetch with `--json`). `docs/WRITERS.md`: the two exit-code conventions (the writer command's per-record codes; `gpconf`'s own 0/1) are stated as different, and the 604/603/606 sentence carries the "270449 appears twice" explanation. Regenerated: every `expected.json` (only titles, coverage text, check descriptions, ambiguity text and generation times changed; a script compared every other field with the committed files; the ambiguity change is four cases whose `mmdot-convention` text still read "301/301" because D-116 changed the wording in `tools/cases.py` without regenerating them, and they now read "304/304"), `manifest.json`, `MANIFEST.md`, every `case.md`, `docs/FAILURES.md` from fresh adapter runs. Adapter counts unchanged: reference 17 exact, naive 14 failing cases with 76 items, python-sgp4 7 with 20.

**D-123 (2026-09-23) — v0.2.1 version DOI 10.5281/zenodo.22926017 (Zenodo record 22926017, created 2026-09-23T21:48:05Z) set in the slots prepared for it (owner instruction): a fourth `identifiers` entry in `CITATION.cff` in place of the comment line, the version-DOI sentence of the README, and the 0.2.1 changelog paragraph that had promised it.** Same sequence as D-106. **D-124 (2026-09-23) — Batch E2, standards slips from audit section 4, each quotation checked against its source.** Sources opened: CCSDS 502.0-B-3 (April 2023) as the PDF at https://ccsds.org/Pubs/502x0b3e1.pdf, text extracted with pdftotext and the pages of Table 4-3 (page 4-6) and annex G (page G-6) looked at as images; the vendored schema files under `schemas/ndmxml-4.0.0/` and `schemas/SOURCE.md`; CelesTrak's GP data formats page read live on 2026-09-24; one HEAD request to the SANA registry. Changes in `docs/RESEARCH.md`: the 4.2.1 quotation carries its two parentheticals again, "(data about data)" and "(explanatory information)" (4.2.1); the 7.3.2 quotation is complete, ending "and spaces (excluding line termination character[s])." (7.3.2); the units rule cites 7.7.1.1 and quotes its four fragments (7.7.1.1 a and b); the SANA schema note quotes each module's header comment separately (master: "This is version 4.0 of the NDM/XML Schema (05/19/2023)." and "This is version 4.0, Rev 0 of the NDM/XML Schema (11/06/2022."; OMM: "This is version 4.0.0 of the NDM/XML Schema (05/19/2023)."; common: "This is version 4.0.0 of the NDM/XML Schema (05/2023)"), the old blended quotation having been the OMM module's line presented as the set's; the companion-schema sentence states what `schemas/SOURCE.md` records (the twelve 2.0.0 files fetched 2026-09-21 with hashes) and that the 3.0.0 master schema named by Figure G-10 resolves (HEAD 2026-09-24: 200, 4,016 bytes, Last-Modified 2023-05-19). Two footnotes: CelesTrak's page still carries "Once the system sends more than 1,000 HTTP 403 errors (and now 301 and 404 errors, too) to an IP address in a day (yes, that happens almost every day), that IP address is put into the firewall and requires manual review to find and remove it." beside the 50-in-2-hours rule the corpus cites; and annex G's note "All of these examples are based on the TLE shown in figure G-6" sits over a TLE with epoch 07064.44075725 while the OMM figures carry 2020-064T10:34:41.4264, so a rendering that reads 20064.44075725 shows the standard's inconsistency. `tools/cases.py`: the check `optional-tle-parameters-may-be-absent` quotes Table 4-3's block heading "(This section is only required if MEAN_ELEMENT_THEORY=SGP/SGP4)" and the NORAD_CAT_ID row's "This keyword is only required if MEAN_ELEMENT_THEORY=SGP/SGP4." and says the standard is ambiguous for SGP/SGP4 messages; the KVN variants case, the manifest and the case documents regenerated. Not changed, contrary to the audit and the instruction that followed it: the GM row. Table 4-3 (page 4-6, seen) marks GM "O"; the "C" is the OPM's Table 3-3 row (page 3-x, extracted text line "GM … km**3/s**2 C" under "Osculating Keplerian Elements"). The row keeps "O" and now says which table says what. Adapter counts unchanged (reference 17 exact, naive 14/76, python-sgp4 7/20); the suite passes.

**D-125 (2026-09-24) — Batch F (audit item 6): one synthetic-derived writer input with a positive-exponent BSTAR, under the D-096 precedent; letters B–S and U–Z stay vectors-only; the nine-digit re-capture is a standing item.** No fetched record carries a BSTAR with a positive exponent (the audit counted 1,206 TLE lines: 1,192 negative exponents, 14 `+0`, none ≥ 1.0), so the form `±NNNNN+E` with E ≥ 1 was untested. The writer case gains one input: SARAMAGO's first-record elements with the catalog number replaced by 99999 and BSTAR by 1.2345, whose TLE field is ` 12345+1`. Both replacements are specification vectors: 99999 is in `vectors/alpha5.json` `below_100000`, is the largest five-digit number, and primary sources describe no catalogued object in 90000–99999 (README; rffit uses it as its placeholder), so the record is not a catalogued object's element set and every writer, the naive one included, renders a full 69-character line for it; 1.2345 is the smallest-digits value of the positive-exponent form. The expected TLE fields are rendered by the corpus (`reference_rendering: corpus-rendered`), the record carries `synthetic_changes` naming both replacements with their sources, and `case_specific.inputs.synthetic_field_vectors` counts it. Checked before adding: the corpus renders ` 12345+1` and reads it back as 1.2345; python-sgp4 2.27's `export_tle` and the naive writer render the same field, so no adapter count moves: reference 17 exact, naive 14 failing cases with 76 items, python-sgp4 7 with 20 (the naive and python-sgp4 writer-case details now say 607 and 4 of 607 where they said 606 and 3 of 606; the catalogue is regenerated). Letters B–S and U–Z stay vectors-only: no real object carries them (the catalog stood at 100789 in July and T is the analyst block), synthetic lines with re-mapped ids would test the corpus's encoder against itself and add nothing `vectors/alpha5.json` does not already prove for every letter, and D-096 admitted synthetic ids only where a refusal is the correct output; the audit's estimate that the B range is years away is [inferred]. Standing item: a second capture of nine-digit SupGP ids at the next launch window, one request through `tools/fetch.py`'s re-capture path, to show the pattern recurs; the site tracker watches presence daily. Real data over synthetic remains the rule; this vector is the one exception the coverage gap justified, and the reader side and the second derivative stay declared as uncovered.

**D-126 (2026-09-24) — The AI-assistance disclosure is removed from the corpus's presentation surfaces (owner instruction); `AUDIT.md`'s limitation sentence is deliberately retained.** README, "How this corpus was built": the framing paragraph now describes the method (phased work, the owner's review at each phase end, the written decision policy in `CLAUDE.md` requiring every non-trivial choice, reversal and correction to be recorded) without naming AI assistance; the audit-trail bullet no longer attributes decisions to "the owner's and the agent's"; the sources bullet drops the sentence about the model's memory of the domain. The independent-audit bullet is kept whole, including its scope qualifier ("performed by a separate AI session … it was not a human review") and its v0.1.0 scope: the owner's rule was to keep the qualifier or drop the audit claim, and the claim was kept because it is the corpus's main verification statement and the qualifier is what makes it honest; dropping the claim would have removed a fact readers rely on, keeping it without the qualifier would have overstated it. CHANGELOG 0.2.1: "a read-only pass by a separate AI session under the maintainer's direction, not the independent audit" becomes "a read-only review pass under the maintainer's direction, distinct from the independent audit", which keeps the distinction D-120 wanted. The kept release-notes draft (private) loses the same phrase; the published GitHub release text for v0.2.1 still carries it and is the owner's to edit. `AUDIT.md` is unchanged: its statement that the audit was performed by a separate AI session and was not a human review stays, because a reader of the audit must know what kind of review it was. Left unchanged and flagged to the owner: `CITATION.cff`'s abstract ("Built with AI assistance under human direction …"), which GitHub's "Cite this repository" and Zenodo's minted records reproduce (the records are immutable per version); `docs/PLAN.md` (exported) and `docs/writeup/post.md`, which describe the assistance historically; `CLAUDE.md` itself, exported and named after the agent; `DECISIONS.md` and the private handoff, which are records, not presentation. The export was regenerated and synced; commit and push are the owner's.

**D-127 (2026-09-24) — Batch G Phase 1: the library survey is compiled and kept privately at `docs/handoff/batch-g-survey-2026-09-24.md`, outside the export allowlist; Phase 2 waits for the owner's review.** Four read-only registry surveys (PyPI, CRAN and CPAN; npm and Packagist; crates.io, Maven Central, pkg.go.dev and Julia General; F-Droid, GitHub topics, tracker apps and reference code), run by research agents on 2026-09-23 and 2026-09-24 under the standing rules (no issue, PR, comment or post; no request to celestrak.org or space-track.org; no installs; a URL for every fact; VERIFIED-in-source or INFERRED on each claim), are compiled into one document: the audit's 22 names reconciled against their rows (all 22 re-found; Stellarium's released 26.2 is inferred to predate the fix on master; libsgp4 has issue #44 open beside PR #42), a candidate index of 215 table rows classed by catalog-number behaviour (A decodes Alpha-5; B keeps the raw string; C fails on an integer parse; D returns a silently wrong identity; E overflows on write; F is OMM-only with an integer of any width; G is a fetch client; H delegates), a 44-entry cross-ecosystem ranking by reach and likelihood of breaking, a de-duplicated count (161 distinct new names beyond the 22, of which 146 carry parsing or writing code and 15 are note-only or fetch-only by the reports' own labels), the toolchain groups Phase 2 needs authorised, and the four agent reports reproduced in the document's section 8 verbatim (headings demoted two levels so they nest under it; text unchanged). Two corrections were made against GitHub before compiling and are recorded with their evidence in the document's section 7: report A's statement that the maintainer opened python-sgp4 issue #169 on 2026-08-28 is wrong (the GitHub API on 2026-09-24: opened 2026-08-20 by michaelbaisch, title "Support OMM NORAD catalog numbers beyond the Alpha-5 range", still open); report D's statement that SatIntel has no TLE parser is wrong (`osint/tle.go` on main, fetched 2026-09-24, splits the lines on whitespace and assigns `strconv.Atoi` at lines 37 and 47 with the error discarded, as report C had said). Four smaller conflicts between the reports (the closing date of Orekit #758, predict4java's GitHub release, go-satellite's last maintainer activity, SatIntel's last commit) are listed and left open; none bears on Phase 2. The file stays private because it carries reach figures and tracker states as fetched on one day, unverified cells marked "?", and inferred rows; publishing any of it would need each row re-verified, and Phase 2's findings notes are the place for verified results. "Viable" for Phase 2 is read as: own catalog-number code (classes A to F and the reference code), runnable here or with a toolchain the owner authorises; delegators and fetch clients are listed but not run unless the owner asks. Alternatives: one merged table with uniform columns (rejected: re-typing about 200 rows risks transcription errors and drops the per-fact URLs; the index plus the verbatim reports keeps both); running the delegators as well (deferred to the owner, one thin adapter each on top of the base library's).

**D-128 (2026-09-24) — Batch G Phase 2, library 1: PyEphem 4.2.1 run against all seventeen cases; findings, scratch adapter, verified patch and outreach draft kept privately at `docs/handoff/batch-g/pyephem/`, outside the export allowlist; nothing filed.** Result: 6 cases fail, 10 skip, 1 pass (`satcat-70000-cutoff`, a data check on the legacy file that involves no adapter; the runner item that follows deals with it). Findings, each reproduced on this Mac with ephem 4.2.1, Python 3.14.6, corpus 30f8d3b: F1 every Alpha-5 catalog field decodes to 0 (`extensions/_libastro.c:2827`, `strtod` stops at the letter; 604 of 604 corpus element sets; the ten element attributes and `compute()` results are identical to the same lines with a five-digit field, tested for 100404 at three instants), a bug, undocumented, and absent from the tracker after eleven searches; F2 seven fields stored in single precision (`libastro/astro.h:323-329`, casts at `dbfmt.c:234-248`; relative error up to 6e-8, so every values item on a TLE file fails), a libastro design choice not raised upstream; F3 the second derivative is not read, a design choice the maintainer confirmed in issue #153; F4 an empty name is refused with "line does not conform to tle format" (`dbfmt.c:222-228`, `_libastro.c:2813-2819`), a misleading message kept as an optional paragraph in the draft; F5 no designator, classification, element set number or ephemeris type exposed, a design choice recorded for the library table. The draft is a new issue because no existing item covers it; it leads with what works, labels every claim, and carries a fourteen-line patch because PyEphem is MIT; the patch was built from the sdist into a second scratch venv and tested on every Alpha-5 boundary, on the corpus (the Alpha-5 case goes from 4 exact and 8 failing items to 8 and 4, the other sixteen cases unchanged) and against PyEphem's own suite (177 tests OK, 4 skipped, on both builds). The adapter passes a name-less 2LE pair the catalog field as its name, because `readtle()` requires a name; recorded in the note. The derived Alpha-5 line quoted as a reproducer is already public (`derived/**/*` is allowlisted). Kept private for the reasons of D-127; the draft moves to `docs/upstream/` only on the owner's word, and the site's library table gets its PyEphem row when the owner decides. Alternatives: raising F2 upstream (rejected: deliberate storage design, harmless for prediction); adding the adapter to `tests/adapters/` now (deferred: scratch adapters stay with their notes until the owner decides which become corpus adapters). **Filed 2026-09-25T02:17:30Z from the owner's account on the owner's word as https://github.com/brandon-rhodes/pyephem/issues/296** (link added under the D-134 convention; prose paragraphs unwrapped for GitHub's line-break rendering, the diff and reproduction fences untouched, the optional closing paragraph on the "line does not conform to tle format" message cut on the owner's instruction, every other word unchanged).

**D-129 (2026-09-24) — `satcat-70000-cutoff` reports `not-exercised` for every parser; the pass it reported was a data check that involved no adapter (owner instruction after the PyEphem run).** The instruction rested on the premise that the reference, naive and python-sgp4 adapters parse SATCAT and would be unaffected. The code says otherwise: `run_satcat` never calls the parser; its one pass/fail item slices the legacy file's id column itself (`satcat-legacy-below-70000`), the six `satcat-record` items were already `info` with the text "SATCAT records are not parsed by the GP parser under test", and none of the three adapters mentions SATCAT. So the change touches every adapter alike: the item is `not-exercised`, its detail keeps the data result and says the parser under test was not involved, and it stays `fail` if the legacy file ever carries an id at or above 70000, as a data property. Counts from the regenerated reports (`tools/_out`, runs of 2026-09-24T20:24Z): reference 16 exact and 1 not exercised (was 17 exact); naive 2 exact, 14 fail, 1 not exercised (was 3 exact, 14 fail), 76 failing items, unchanged; python-sgp4 1 exact, 7 within tolerance, 7 fail, 1 skip, 1 not exercised (was 2 exact), 20 failing items, unchanged. The failing counts the README, changelog and site quote (14 and 7 of 17) do not move; the reference control line does, and README line 97, the changelog's [Unreleased] section and the catalogue's header and legend now say so; `docs/FAILURES.md` was regenerated (its header wording, timestamp and SATCAT row are the only changes); the case's interpretation text in `tools/make_manifest.py` gained a sentence, and `case.md`, `MANIFEST.md` and `manifest.json` were regenerated. Test: `SatcatCaseTests` asserts, for the reference and naive adapters and for python-sgp4 when importable, that the case carries no pass, pass-tolerance or fail item, is `not-exercised` when the legacy file is on disk and `skip` (source-present only) when it is not; suite 119, OK. Observed on the way: the case lists `catalog-number-is-integer` among its checks, but `run_satcat` evaluates nothing under that name. The site's status table (`site/content/failures.json`, the case's row) shows `pass` three times and needs an S-entry at the next site update. Alternatives: an `info` item (rejected: a case with only info items reports `skip`, which reads as "not run"); a `parse_satcat` hook so an adapter could earn the pass (deferred: a protocol change nobody asked for). Left uncommitted for the owner: the premise did not hold and the reference control line is public text.

**D-130 (2026-09-24) — PR #172 merged upstream as 8126f77 and #171 closed: the README's upstream status paragraph and the pull-request record say so; the python-sgp4 counts stay against 2.27.** Verified from the GitHub API on 2026-09-24 before editing: the pull request is merged (merged_at 2026-09-24T12:58:27Z, merged by brandon-rhodes, merge commit 8126f77 with parent bf25b00, head 0f20787), master is identical to 8126f77, issue #171 is closed at 12:58:28Z with state_reason completed, and PyPI's latest sgp4 is 2.27 of 2026-07-03. Changed: the README's "Upstream" paragraph (dated 2026-09-24; the bullet now records the merge, the commit, the closed issue and that no release carries the fix, so the 7-of-17 result stands against 2.27) and the status block of `docs/upstream/pr171-pull-request.md` (the two amendments and the merge). Not changed: `docs/FAILURES.md`, the case documents and the site's failures table, which report runs against 2.27 and change only when a release with the fix is installed and run; the changelog, which records corpus changes, not upstream ones; the fork clone, whose branch `omm-empty-object-id` at 0f20787 is now merged upstream and can be deleted or kept as the owner prefers. The site's matching edits are S-036. Alternatives: re-running the python-sgp4 adapter against master to show the case passing (deferred: the corpus reports released versions, and the site's weekly job will show the flip when a release ships).

**D-131 (2026-09-24) — A catalog id the runner cannot take as an integer is reported in the values item, with the value and its type, instead of the runner raising inside the parse item (owner instruction after the satellite.js run).** Before: `norm_record` called `int()` on every integer field, so an adapter returning the raw TLE field `'A0000'` made the case's file fail with "parser raised ValueError: invalid literal for int() with base 10: 'A0000'", which reads as a crash in the adapter and hides what the library returned; the satellite.js adapter worked around it by returning no id. Now `as_integer()` converts what converts losslessly (an int, a digit string, an integral float or Decimal; a digit string still gets the type note that the `catalog-number-is-integer` check reports) and returns a problem text for the rest (a bool, anything `int()` refuses, a fractional float or Decimal); `norm_record` leaves such a field `None` and records the problem in the record's `_bad` map; `compare_values` reports "N record(s) whose norad_cat_id is not an integer, e.g. 'A0000' (str)" separately from records that simply carry no id, and "id X: rev_at_epoch is not an integer: 12.5 (float, fractional)" for the other integer fields; the integer check names up to three examples. `tle-catalog-field-decodes` is unchanged (a field with no integer is "not decoded"). Tests: `NonIntegerCatalogIdTests` (the conversion table, and the reference reader with the TLE field kept as text run on `alpha5-tle-derived`: no parse item, the values item names `'A0000' (str)`). Counts for the three adapters are unchanged (they return integers). Reason: several remaining Phase 2 candidates keep the field as a string (satellite.js, tle.js, the raw-string keepers), and the report must say what they returned. Alternative: converting digit strings and rejecting everything else silently (rejected: the type is part of the finding).

**D-132 (2026-09-24) — Batch G Phase 2, library 2: satellite.js 7.1.0 run against all seventeen cases; findings, scratch adapter, two verified patches and a one-issue draft kept privately at `docs/handoff/batch-g/satellitejs/`, outside the export allowlist; nothing filed.** Result: 7 cases fail, 7 skip, 1 pass, 1 within tolerance, 1 not exercised; all 604 TLE records read with every element right to 3e-16, nine-digit OMM ids taken without a cap. Findings, each reproduced on this Mac with satellite.js 7.1.0 under Node 26.5.0 through the runner's `--cmd` protocol: F1 the TLE reader keeps the catalog field as the raw string (`src/io.ts:86`, typed `satnum: string`) and the OMM reader returns the same object's id as a different string (`io.ts:197`), with no decoder in the library, so 0 of 604 Alpha-5 fields have an integer form, a design choice with a gap; F2 `json2satrec` builds the epoch through a JavaScript `Date` (`io.ts:199-208`) and keeps milliseconds only, so 571 of the corpus's 574 OMM JSON records came back truncated by up to 999 µs, measured as 0.92 m along track at epoch + 30 min for a 104 µs shift and 6.19 m for 808 µs, a bug the library's own source comment contradicts; F3 no checksum or line-consistency check, F4 no designator, classification, element set or revolution number, F5 `satrec.no` is the un-Kozai'd value (`nokozai` holds the parsed one), F6 a missing OMM key gives NaN, all design choices or outside the cases. The F2 patch (source and test, against tag 7.1.0) keeps the digits beyond the third; verified on the corpus (every OMM epoch item passes; only the Alpha-5 case still fails) and on the library's own suite (403 of 403 with one existing test rebuilt to append `Z` instead of round-tripping a `Date`, plus a new test that fails without the change; `test:types` and `biome check` clean; the four wasm test files fail before and after for want of the wasm build). The draft is one issue with two sections, the epoch bug and its patch first and the identity question with a boundary-tested decoder sketch second, on the owner's decision that a maintainer reads one issue from a stranger better than two in the same hour; the tracker had nothing on either (nine queries). The adapter passes the library's raw string through since D-131. **Filed 2026-09-24T21:40:16Z from the owner's account on the owner's word as https://github.com/shashwatak/satellite-js/issues/185** (link added under the D-134 convention; prose paragraphs unwrapped for GitHub's line-break rendering, the four fences and every word unchanged). **Pull requests from the owner's fork on the owner's word, after the maintainer (1valdis) asked for both on #185 on 2026-09-25: the epoch fix 2026-09-25T22:17:34Z as https://github.com/shashwatak/satellite-js/pull/186 (the verified patch rebased onto develop 95e43b7, with a changelog line; `vitest --project js` 410 to 411, types and Biome clean; the new test fails without the change) and the decoder 2026-09-25T22:34:52Z as https://github.com/shashwatak/satellite-js/pull/187 (`alpha5ToNumber` in `io.ts` as the maintainer suggested, exported and pinned, boundary tests, 410 to 415; it adds the decoder and does not change what `twoline2satrec` returns, the identity question left to the maintainers; the two merge cleanly in either order, 416 combined).** Alternative: two separate issues (rejected by the owner).

**D-133 (2026-09-24) — Batch G Phase 2, library 3: Gpredict 2.6's SGP4 module run against all seventeen cases; findings, harness, GLib stand-in and a standalone issue draft kept privately at `docs/handoff/batch-g/gpredict/`, outside the export allowlist; no Alpha-5 outreach, the site's ecosystem list instead (S-037); nothing filed.** What was built: `src/sgpsdp/` only, unmodified, compiled with clang against a two-file stand-in for GLib (`g_ascii_strtod` as `strtod` in the C locale) behind a harness of my own that calls `Get_Next_Tle_Set()`; Gpredict's own `test-001` builds and runs the same way; the GTK application and its updater were not built (GLib and GTK absent), so their behaviour is inferred from the source. Result: 6 cases fail, 10 skip, 1 not exercised, 0 pass. F1 every Alpha-5 field decodes to 0 through `atoi` (`sgp_in.c:115-117`; the checksum counts a letter as 0 and `Good_Elements` only matches the two fields), all 604 corpus sets; in the application the `.sat` files and the updater's hash are keyed by that number (`gtk-sat-data.c:64`, `compat.c:339`, `tle-update.c:1001, 1023`) [inferred]. Upstream, verified from the thread: PR #412 (2026-07-18) added a decoder at both sites and was closed the same day by its author as not meant for upstream, withdrawn rather than declined; the maintainer replied on 2026-07-19 that he was not aware of Alpha-5, that CelesTrak would not, as far as he could tell, support it, and that CelesTrak CSV support is in progress (#250 open since 2021). The corpus's own case agrees with his view of CelesTrak, whose TLE output omits objects above 99999, so a stock Gpredict on the default feeds is missing those objects, not misnumbering them. Owner's instruction: no draft for this; the site's ecosystem entry states the three facts and the practical position. F2 the mean motion loses its last digit on every record: `Convert_Satellite_Data` copies ten characters of the eleven-character field (`sgp_in.c:221-223`), so 16.05064833 reads as 16.0506483; measured with python-sgp4 on the ISS set with its last digit dropped, 0.04 m at 1 h, 0.86 m at 24 h, 6.0 m at 7 days, worst case 4.5 times; inherited from sgp4sdp4-0.3 through Hari Nair's version, and Xplanet's copy (`src/libsgp4sdp4/sgp_in.c:154`, Debian 1.3.1-5) carries the same line; no tracker item (fourteen queries; #47 of 2017 was a bad B* value). The fix is two numbers (copy count and terminator index, 10 to 11); verified on a scratch copy: the four TLE-bearing gp cases pass and `test-001` still runs, its own `test-001.tle` mean motion now read whole. Drafted on the owner's instruction as a standalone issue with no mention of Alpha-5 or the corpus, in prose without quoting a Gpredict line (GPL-2.0; no Gpredict code enters the corpus); **filed 2026-09-24T21:37:56Z from the owner's account on the owner's word as https://github.com/csete/gpredict/issues/426** (link added under the D-134 convention; paragraphs unwrapped for GitHub's line-break rendering, words unchanged). F3 `epoch_year = 2000 + yy` ("we assume > 2000", `sgp_in.c:147`) affects only the info dialog, propagation uses the pivot at 57 in `Julian_Date_of_Epoch`; F4 eccentricity clamped at 1e-6 (issue #156); F5 strict layout checks; F6 no classification, raw designator, a name line required. The harness supplies a placeholder name for name-less 2LE pairs and does not report it. Alternatives: building the GTK application (rejected: toolchain absent, and the parser module is where the finding lives); a combined Alpha-5-plus-mean-motion report (rejected by the owner: the mean-motion report stands on its own).

**D-134 (2026-09-24) — Convention for Batch G, recorded in `CLAUDE.md` (owner instruction): a decision entry about an unreported finding names the library and that a finding was recorded, and keeps the specifics in the private handoff until the report is filed; once filed, the entry gains the link.** Reason: `DECISIONS.md` is on the export allowlist, so D-128, D-132 and D-133 published the PyEphem, satellite.js and Gpredict findings in summary before their drafts were filed. Those three entries preceded the convention and are left as written, their drafts going out the week of 2026-09-24; from D-135 on, an entry for a library run says which library, that findings were recorded under `docs/handoff/batch-g/<library>/`, and nothing that would let a reader reconstruct the defect before the maintainer has it. Adding the filed link later is the one in-place edit the append-only rule admits, since it completes the entry without changing the decision. Alternative: rewriting the three entries now (rejected by the owner: the drafts go out this week, and published history is not rewritten).

**D-141 (2026-09-24) — Batch G Phase 2 complete: eight libraries run, in the owner's order (PyEphem, satellite.js, Gpredict, gods-eye-view, SatDump, libsgp4, tle.js, astroz), one report each, every finding reproduced by running the code, nothing filed by the agent.** The closing table (library, version, cases failed, most serious finding) is kept in the handoff under the D-134 convention until the reports are filed; the three reports already filed are D-132, D-133 and D-135. Filing queue, all on the owner's word and from the owner's account: the PyEphem issue (D-128), the gods-eye-view issue (D-136), the SatDump report (D-137), the libsgp4 comment and issue (D-138, in the morning batch with PyEphem), the tle.js issue then its pull request (D-139), and the two astroz issues (D-140). Once each is filed, its entry gains the link in place. **Filed the same night, 2026-09-24T23:41Z to 2026-09-25T00:05Z, everything in that queue but PyEphem and the tle.js pull request: eight postings, their links in D-136 to D-140.**

**D-142 (2026-09-25) — A gate over this month's launches, computed from existing cases, added to the runner's summary and JSON report; not a new case.** The question: given the objects launched in the last 30 days, as a provider serves them, does the parser return them with the right identity? Inputs already in the corpus: the CelesTrak CSV capture of the last-30-days group (256 objects, ids 100404-100789, captured 2026-09-21) and the corpus's Alpha-5 rendering of those records, the form Space-Track's TLE output carries; the CSV case also records that CelesTrak's TLE request for the group answers HTTP 404. Per format the parser reads, records count as loaded (returned with the expected integer id), misidentified (returned with an id that is absent, not an integer, or not the expected one: 0, NaN, a raw string) or dropped (no record came back, whether refused or silently skipped); the eight Batch G runs showed all three shapes. Mechanics: `compare_values` now records structured counts on every values item (`expected`, `returned`, `loaded`, `misidentified`, `dropped`, `non_integer_id`), so the gate reads numbers rather than sentences; `gpconf/gates.py` holds the gate's definition and reads the snapshot's date, count, id range and leading letters from the case data; the CLI prints `gate this month's launches: ...` below the case table and the JSON report carries `gates`. Wording, on the owner's instruction, names the behaviour and never grades the project, since the site's table says a count is not a verdict: "reads this month's launches in every format it reads here", "only via CSV", "not in any format it reads", and for a TLE-only parser "nothing to load from this feed", with Space-Track's Alpha-5 form reported alongside; a parser that raises on the file counts as dropping every record; a format the parser does not read says "not read", a file not on disk "file absent". Every headline carries the snapshot facts and the caveat that a decoder wrong from J upward is not caught by a snapshot whose ids all begin with A. Computed from the Batch G records without re-running, the eight libraries read: PyEphem, satellite.js, Gpredict, tle.js: nothing to load from the CelesTrak TLE feed, 256 misidentified as Space-Track serves them; gods-eye-view: 1 misidentified, 255 dropped; SatDump 1.2.2: 256 dropped, master: only via CSV; libsgp4 master: only via CSV, PR #42: reads them in TLE; astroz: reads them in TLE, with the letter caveat. Tests: the four wordings, the no-grade rule, the snapshot facts from data, and the reference and naive adapters through a run (132 tests). Alternatives: a new case (rejected: the inputs and checks exist, and a case would double-count in the seventeen-case table); grading words LOADS/PARTLY/NO (rejected by the owner: a verdict on the project). Stage two, a separate decision: JSON, XML and KVN renderings of the same snapshot, so readers of those formats get a measured value.

**D-143 (2026-09-25) — The provider's recorded empty answer is handed to the parser under test as its own check, `empty-answer-yields-no-records`: zero records and no error is the pass, an exception or an invented record fails, a parser without the format skips.** Reason (the second of the four ideas under review, fail-closed semantics): a parser must keep three outcomes apart, a value it cannot represent, a file it cannot read, and an endpoint with no data; the corpus tested the first as a required refusal (the writer case, the vectors) and applied all three to itself in Batch A, but the third was never presented to a parser, because `load_source` recognised the 404 body and returned an empty result without calling the parser. Now, in the four cases that hold such a body (`analyst-objects`, `nine-digit-supgp-launch-nominals`, `six-digit-omm-saramago`, `tle-omits-six-digit-objects`; six files, five `No GP data found` and one `No SupGP data found`), the body goes to the parser first; the runner's own empty-404 handling then continues as before. Declared once in `tools/cases.py` and attached to the four cases' check lists; manifest and the four case docs regenerated; the frozen `expected.json` files embed their check lists from the last expected-value regeneration and gain the id at the next one, which does not affect the runner (it reads the manifest). Counts: the reference, naive and python-sgp4 adapters each return an empty list for the body, so each gains pass items and no failing count moves; the per-case rows of `docs/FAILURES.md` are unchanged. In CI's offline runs the raw files are absent, so the check does not run there. Tests: the four cases declare the check; the reference and naive adapters pass it on every body on disk; a parser that raises fails with "not an error" in the detail; a parser that returns a record fails; a parser without the format skips. Not done, held for their own decisions: a per-record refusal channel (item 2 of the idea) and a corrupt-input case (item 3, synthetic-derived).

**D-144 (2026-09-25) — A refusal channel in the adapter protocol, so that a record the library refuses with an error is kept apart from one it drops silently; the built-in python-sgp4 adapter adopts it; the eight Batch G runs are not re-run and the site's table carries a footnote.** Form (owner-approved as proposed): an entry in the list the adapter already returns, carrying `_refused` with the library's reason as a non-empty string, optionally `_field` (the catalog field as the input carried it) and `_input` (up to 80 characters of the line); and an optional first entry `{"_adapter": {"refusals": true}}` declaring that every record dropped with an error is reported, which is what makes "dropped" honest: with the declaration zero refusals means dropped silently, without it the report says refusals were not reported. Owner's addition: the reason is required and non-empty; a refusal without one is counted as a drop and fails the file's `refusals` item, since a refusal that gives no reason is no better than a drop for the thing this change separates. Runner: refusals are split out at the one normalisation point before any check sees them; `compare_values` credits a refusal whose field decodes to an expected id (digits or Alpha-5, decoded by the corpus) and writes "N expected record(s) not returned: R refused with a reason (e.g. ...), S silently"; the counts gain `refused`, `refused_matched`, `refused_without_reason`, `refusals_reported` and `top_refusal_reason`, and `dropped` excludes matched refusals; a per-file `refusals` item lists reasons with counts (info, or fail when a reason is missing); the empty-answer check (D-143) fails a refusal of the 404 body, since an empty answer is not an unreadable one; the gate reports "R refused (reason)" and "D dropped silently" or "D dropped (refusals not reported by this adapter)". Adoption: one line in a harness's catch block; the python-sgp4 adapter now catches `ValueError` from `twoline2rv` and from `omm.initialize` per record and declares the channel, so its nine-digit result reads as a refusal with the library's own message rather than a whole-file parse failure; no failing case count moves. Not re-run, on the owner's instruction: the eight Batch G harnesses, whose recorded "dropped" counts may include refusals (libsgp4 master's `TleException`, astroz's `InvalidCharacter`, Gpredict's rejected sets); the site's hand-run table says so in a footnote (S-043). Where the library returns a value rather than an error (satellite.js, tle.js, PyEphem's 0) or drops inside itself with no signal (SatDump's registry, gods-eye-view's dedupe) there is nothing to adopt, and misidentified or dropped is the finding. Tests: the split and the reason rule, field decoding, refusals credited and named through a run, the undeclared adapter not credited with silence, the reasonless refusal counted as a drop, the reference reporting none, the refused 404 body failing, and the python-sgp4 adapter's nine-digit refusal (144 tests). Fail-closed rule added when the first regeneration showed the hole: with the adapter refusing the one record in `csv-json-omitted-mandatory-fields`' two SupGP files instead of failing the file, the parser returned zero records and every per-file check passed on nothing, flipping the case to pass; now a file the reference reads records from, for which the parser returned none, gets a failing `records-returned` item that names the refusals or the silent drops, with the same counts the gate reads, and no other check runs on it (the same discipline as D-113 for the reference reader). With it the failing case counts are unchanged: reference none, naive 14, python-sgp4 7. One item count moves and is meant to: python-sgp4's nine-digit case has five failing items instead of four, because `starlink-all.csv` now loads its five-digit records around the 27 refused ones and the classification-C check runs on it and finds C not preserved, a result the whole-file failure had hidden. Held for its own decision: item 3, a corrupt-input case.

**D-145 (2026-09-25) — v0.3.0 planned as the fail-closed release; the plan is a private note at `docs/handoff/v0.3.0-plan.md`, outside the export allowlist; nothing built, no date.** Shape: D-142 to D-144 are already on main (the gate and structured counts, the provider's empty answer handed to the parser, the refusal channel with a required reason and fail-closed on a file returned empty); the release's remaining new work is one case, corrupt input, as a fourth input class feeding the same four counts, loaded, misidentified, refused, dropped, rather than a new concept, and the case count moves from seventeen to eighteen in the same release, because the protocol additions already call for a minor bump under the README's rule and the count change then costs one rebuild of everything that carries it instead of two. The case tests the parser under test on input the corpus has never fed it, where D-113 and D-117 hardened the corpus's own readers; it checks behaviours the Batch G reports recorded as documented but unverified on corrupt input. It needs the owner's provenance approval for synthetic-derived inputs under the D-096 precedent, each a frozen real record with one stated edit, placed between valid records so that resynchronisation is measured. Downstream: the version constants, the site's count sentences and data files, release notes, the Zenodo description, the failures table, and eight Batch G re-runs against the new shape producing a second round of findings and second contacts with the maintainers, which the note argues are the point of the release. The note marks every unverified claim. Alternatives: a corrupt-input case as its own concept with its own vocabulary (rejected: the D-144 counts already describe every outcome it can produce); adding the case before or after the protocol release (rejected: two rebuilds of the count).

**D-146 (2026-09-25) — Identity over time stated as an open question in the coverage gaps, not answered and not a new section (the fourth of the four ideas under review, documentation only).** A nine-digit SupGP id is a launch nominal CelesTrak serves for the days between launch and 18 SDS's first GP data, after which the object is expected under a catalog number of 100000 or above; what a tracker keeps as its stable key across that change, and how the nominal's history merges into the catalogued object's, is a question the corpus cannot answer. Checked first, so the text extends what was there: the nine-digit and classification-C cases already call their files perishable, the research note holds the two CelesTrak quotations the inference rests on, and it recorded, without interpreting, that 72000 and 799501621 share OBJECT_ID 2026-219A. Two facts checked for the text from the frozen records: the corpus holds no object in both forms (the 27 nominals are launch 2026-219; the same day's last-30-days group lists launches 2026-156 to 2026-220 but not 2026-219), and the shared designator is not one-to-one (72000 and 72001 carry 2026-219A and 2026-219B, as do two per-satellite nominals). Placed where the other gaps live: a gap entry on the nine-digit case, a one-sentence cross-reference on the classification-C case, both in `tools/cases.py` with the case docs and manifest regenerated, and the README's existing nine-digit bullet extended. The text says what the corpus covers (both forms parse; the nine-digit case is perishable and tied to a launch window), what it does not (correlation, merging, key stability), and why that is out of scope rather than missing: a data-relationship question for the provider's documentation, not a parsing property a fixture can freeze. Every claim in it is marked tested or inferred; the "no way to map" sentence in CelesTrak's text is about the TLE field width and is not used. No code changed.

**D-147 (2026-09-25) — Propagation-equivalence grading (grading two TLE renderings by whether they propagate to the same TEME position within a tolerance) is not built; an optional information-only figure in metres is left as a stated possibility. The fourth of the four ideas under review, closed.** Premise corrected in review: the idea as put said the writer case grades bytes. It does not. `tle-writer-round-trip` reads the written lines back through the corpus's own reference reader and accepts each field at its own resolution, quantised by truncation or by rounding half up (D-070, D-071), and `tle-writer-matches-provider-rendering` reports byte-identity for information only. The "two writers, different but equally valid TLEs" problem the idea addressed is therefore already handled for every freedom the format is known to allow. That correction came from the agent's review of the proposal, not from the owner. What settles the decision, measured on 2026-09-25 with python-sgp4 2.27 (WGS72, mode `i`) on the corpus's frozen ISS record (25544, epoch 2026-09-20T12:42:37.141056), no network: each TLE field moved by one unit in its last printed digit, position difference against the unmoved record at the same instant, in metres:

| field, one unit in its last digit | epoch | +1 h | +1 day | +7 days |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| mean anomaly (1e-4 deg) | 11.861 | 11.856 | 11.872 | 11.870 |
| argument of perigee (1e-4 deg) | 11.872 | 11.852 | 11.861 | 11.859 |
| RA of ascending node (1e-4 deg) | 11.870 | 9.274 | 11.858 | 11.800 |
| inclination (1e-4 deg) | 0.020 | 9.426 | 3.298 | 23.179 |
| epoch (1e-8 day) | 6.616 | 6.613 | 6.622 | 6.621 |
| eccentricity (1e-7) | 0.769 | 1.299 | 0.745 | 0.678 |
| mean motion (1e-8 rev/day) | 0.003 | 0.018 | 0.428 | 2.999 |
| BSTAR (1e-8, last mantissa digit of 0.15975e-3) | 0.000 | 0.000 | 0.237 | 11.661 |
| first and second derivatives of mean motion | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

(1) A tolerance that accepts every valid writer must admit one unit in an angle's last digit, about 12 m at every horizon and up to 23 m at a week: a floor of roughly 12 m, derivable per record from its own resolution, not a guess.
