(2) The three precision defects the corpus has found are all smaller than that floor: satellite.js's millisecond epoch truncation, 6.19 m at 30 minutes for an 808 µs loss (D-132); Gpredict's dropped mean-motion digit, 0.04 m at 1 h, 0.86 m at 24 h and 6.0 m at 7 days (D-133); libsgp4's epoch rounding, 8 µs, about 6 cm along track (D-138, reported in dnwrnr/sgp4#45). A propagation grade wide enough for valid writers passes all three; the field-resolution checks catch them. (3) Propagation is blind by construction to identity and to both derivatives: in python-sgp4 at 8126f77, `sgp4()` in `sgp4/propagation.py` never reads `ndot` or `nddot` and `sgp4init` only stores them (the zero row above confirms it), and no identity field (catalog number, international designator, classification, element set number, name) enters the propagation at all. The eight Batch G findings were identity and epoch bugs; propagation grading would have missed every identity finding and, by (2), the epoch findings too. Also weighed: the runner is standard-library only (README), so the dependency would be required (breaking that for every user and CI), optional (reports differing by machine) or vendored (numerical code to maintain); and python-sgp4 is under test here, so using its propagator would put code from a tested project into the oracle, although for a relative comparison of two renderings a shared propagator error largely cancels, and an absolute claim would need a second implementation or published SGP4 verification vectors, whose terms are unread [untested]. Stated possibility, not built: an information-only figure in metres (the propagated position difference at epoch, +1 day and +7 days) on the writer case and on `tle-vs-omm-precision-loss`, skipping visibly when SGP4 is not installed, never passed or failed; worth doing only if the site wants to show metres. Alternatives: propagation grading replacing field-resolution grading (rejected by (1) to (3)); alongside it as pass/fail (rejected: at the tolerance valid writers require it can fail nothing the existing checks pass).

**D-148 (2026-09-26) — Statuses the runner reported without having earned them, in the spirit of Batch A: a provider file that is not on disk reports `not-fetched`, never `skip`; the count line says how many cases need fetched data and names a case that ran on part of its files; the fetch command names a script that exists; and the gate's headline claims nothing about a format it never tried, dates its snapshot from the frozen capture, and judges a freshly fetched CSV against its own count. The last of these is the one to remember: the gate would have shown the corpus's own reference reader as unable to read CSV on the first fresh fetch by any public user. No published count moved.** Found while measuring what a pip-installed runner would show before fetching (the v0.3 packaging proposal): the public export, which carries no provider data, run offline with the three built-in adapters. Five faults, all reproduced before the change. (1) A case whose provider files were absent reported `skip`, the status the site's library page defines as meaning "the library has no reader for the case's format"; python-sgp4's offline run printed 14 skips, one of them a real missing KVN reader and 13 missing data under the same word. D-119 had moved these cases from `not-exercised` to `skip` for the same reason, that the word claimed something about the data; `skip` in turn claimed something about the parser. (2) A case that ran on part of its files passed without a word: after a first fetch the three re-capture files are not yet on disk, and `analyst-objects`, `bstar-and-derivative-forms` and `tle-omits-six-digit-objects` reported `pass` with the gap visible only as a skip count in a column the default output does not explain. (3) The missing-file detail and the drift messages said "run tools/fetch.py", a path relative to a clone's root, which a user outside the root or with an installed runner does not have. (4) The gate headline said "reads this month's launches in every format it reads here" when the CSV capture was absent, and for a parser that misread the TLE lines it said "nothing to load from this feed", a claim that the parser reads TLE only, when its CSV reading had never been tried; a run selected with `--case` did the same, since the CSV case was not run. CI's offline naive run printed that TLE-only headline on every push. (5) Two faults in the gate found while fixing (4), not in the proposal: its snapshot date came from the local fetch's metadata, falling back to `expected.json`'s generation date, so an offline run printed 2026-09-23 for a capture of 2026-09-21 and a fresh fetch would print the user's fetch date beside the frozen count; and every format was judged against the frozen 256, so a CSV fetched on a later day was misjudged. Simulated in a scratch copy with ten rows removed from the CSV (246 records, live tier): the reference reader loaded 246 of 246 and the headline said "only via TLE". The last-30-days group is a rolling window, and the site's tracker, which fetches it daily, recorded 256 records at the frozen capture and then 231 later on 2026-09-21, 203 on 2026-09-23, 228 on 2026-09-24 and 204 on 2026-09-25 (its fetch of 2026-09-22 failed): never 256 again. So on the first fresh fetch by any public user the gate would have shown the corpus's own reference reader, the control every other result is read against, as unable to read CSV. The published results never showed it only because they are run on the original capture. Changes: `runner.py` gains the status `not-fetched`, ranked above `skip` and below `not-exercised` in a case's status, a `not-fetched` key in every case's counts, `CaseResult.missing()`, `fetch_hint(root)` (the clone's own script by a path that works from the working directory, quoted when it holds a space, or the repository URL when the root has no script), `is_provider_data(path)` (a file under `fixtures/<case>/raw/`) and `CorpusIncomplete`: a missing file that ships with the corpus (`derived/`, `vectors/`) cannot be fetched, so "need fetched data" would be false; the run stops with exit status 2 instead, and the per-case handler lets the error through rather than recording it as a parser failure. `__main__.py` adds the `n/f` column, "N need fetched data" in the count line, a line naming cases with no provider files, a line naming cases that ran on part of theirs (with the re-capture timing when one is missing) and the fetch command. `gates.py`: the states `not fetched` and `not run` limit the claim to "every format measured here", "only via X of the formats measured here" or "not in any format measured here", with the format left out named before the numbers, and forbid the TLE-only headline; `not read`, a format the parser declared unsupported, is a fact about the parser and keeps the old wording; the snapshot date is the source's `retrieved_at` in `expected.json`; each format is judged against its own values item's expected count, and a live capture is labelled "live capture fetched <date>, N objects, not the snapshot", or "every record dropped" when the parser raised on it. Two judgement calls, confirmed by the owner on 2026-09-26: a case that ran on part of its files keeps the result of those files and is named below the count line, rather than reporting `not-fetched` over its passes, because the passes are real results on the files present and a failure among them must still show; and a missing file that ships with the corpus stops the run with exit status 2. Alternatives: keeping `skip` and changing only the detail text (rejected: the site's tables read the status, not the detail); reporting a missing shipped file as `not-fetched` or as a parser failure (rejected: the first gives a false instruction, the second charges the parser for a broken copy). What moved, measured: in the full-data runs of reference, naive and python-sgp4 every case status, count, item and gate headline is identical before and after (compared field by field); `docs/FAILURES.md` differs only in its generation time; each case's JSON counts gain `"not-fetched": 0`. The site: its findings table is built from those full-data runs and does not move; its hand-run table's skips were checked against the pattern a missing-data case leaves, a skip count equal to the case's source count, and the 27 rows that match it are TLE-only libraries facing the all-XML `omm-xml-schema` or the all-CSV-and-JSON `csv-json-omitted-mandatory-fields`, whose 20 files were on disk from 2026-09-21, three days before the hand runs, so every skip there is a missing reader and the site's definition holds. Offline, the public export: reference 4 pass and 13 skip becomes 4 pass and 13 need fetched data; naive 4 fail and 13 skip becomes 4 fail and 13 need fetched data; python-sgp4 1 within tolerance, 2 fail and 14 skip becomes the same with 1 skip and 13 need fetched data; the naive gate headline changes from "nothing to load from this feed" to "not in any format measured here (CSV not measured: not fetched)". Tests: `tests/test_not_fetched.py` (new: the status, the JSON counts, the count line and table header, skip kept for a parser without hooks, a partly fetched case run from a scratch root without its re-capture, the fetch command in a clone, with a space and without the script, and exit 2 for a missing shipped file); `tests/test_gates.py` (every-format and not-in-any-format headlines with a format not fetched or not run, no TLE-only claim then, the not-read wording unchanged, only-via with an unknown third format, a live capture judged on its own count, no borrowed count when the parser raised on a live file, and the snapshot date read from `expected.json` with and without the local metadata); `tests/test_runner.py` (the D-119 tests now expect `not-fetched`). 162 tests pass in the private repository; in a copy of the public export with the new code, 160 run, 150 pass and 10 skip for want of provider data, and CI's two offline commands exit 0 and 1 as before. README ("Tolerances" and the gate paragraph), CHANGELOG and the CI comment updated.

**D-149 (2026-09-26) — v0.3.0 re-planned as the packaging release and the corrupt-input case moved to v0.4.0 (owner decision); the plan split into two private notes; two corrections recorded, one to D-145's argument and one to the proposal's own count.** After an outside review concluded that the corpus's ceiling is adoption friction, not missing cases, the agent proposed packaging on 2026-09-26 and the owner accepted it. v0.3.0 is: a PyPI package, `gpconf`, whose runner stays standard-library only, with optional extras; a `--preset` flag with four presets pip can complete (reference, naive, sgp4, pyephem) and two that need Node (satellite.js, tle.js); the five compiled and application harnesses (libsgp4, Gpredict, SatDump, astroz, gods-eye-view) kept out of the wheel as recipes; and an offline, report-only GitHub Action that exits 0 and carries no badge. The case count stays at seventeen. The corrupt-input case moves to v0.4.0 with D-145's decisions 2 to 4; decision 1, the README rule's added words "or an additive protocol change", stays with v0.3.0, which carries the protocol change it covers. The note D-145 names, `docs/handoff/v0.3.0-plan.md`, is renamed `v0.4.0-plan.md` and revised; a new `v0.3.0-plan.md` holds the packaging plan: the wheel's contents and exclusions (no raw provider data, D-019; no SupGP values, D-049; built from the public export, never from the private repository, whose `expected.json` files carry the values the export scrubs); the runner's root taken from its own file path, which points at site-packages once installed and is to be split into a read-only corpus root and a writable data root; a per-user cache folder for fetched data; the Node resolution problem, marked inferred and untested; and the Action's reason for staying offline, a fetch pattern from shared runner addresses that CelesTrak has not agreed to. Both notes stay private. Corrections, both from the agent: D-145's note argued that adding the case after a protocol-only 0.3.0 would force the rebuild of everything that carries the count twice; it does not, because v0.3.0 keeps seventeen cases and those surfaces change only when the count does, so the cost of landing later is one more release cycle, not a second rebuild. And the packaging proposal said four of the eight Batch G re-runs would become preset commands; three will (PyEphem, satellite.js, tle.js), since python-sgp4, the fourth pip preset, is a built-in adapter and not one of the eight. Alternatives: packaging and the case in one v0.3.0 (rejected: the case is unbuilt and needs its own provenance approval, and packaging should not wait for it); packaging as a 0.2.x patch (rejected: main already carries an additive protocol change, which makes the next release a minor).

**D-150 (2026-09-26) — v0.3.0 packaging, stage 1: the runner works when installed, not only from a clone. Two roots, a per-user cache folder for provider data with a `--data` flag and a `GPCONF_DATA` variable, and the fetch script as the `gpconf fetch` subcommand; no packaging metadata, build, upload or Action (owner instruction). A correction to D-148's re-capture sentence.** The problem, from the plan (`docs/handoff/v0.3.0-plan.md`, section 4): `__main__.py` and `runner.py` took the corpus root as the folder above the package, which is the repository in a clone and site-packages once installed; and the fetch script existed only as `tools/fetch.py`, a path an installed copy does not have. Changes. `gpconf/locate.py` (new) resolves two roots. The corpus root, read-only, holds what ships (manifest, every `expected.json` and `case.md`, `derived/`, `vectors/`, `tools/fetchlist.json`); first match of `--root`, a clone (the folder above the package holds `manifest.json` and `fixtures/`), and the corpus bundled as package data in `gpconf/corpus/`, found through `importlib.resources` so that an import from an archive is refused with a reason rather than failing on a missing file. The data root holds the provider files in the clone's layout, `fixtures/<case>/raw/`; first match of `--data`, `GPCONF_DATA`, the corpus root itself when that is a clone or came from `--root` (every clone and every test keeps its layout), and a per-user cache folder, one per corpus version: `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/gpconf/<version>` or `~/.cache/gpconf/<version>` (a relative `XDG_CACHE_HOME` is ignored, as the XDG specification says), `~/Library/Caches/gpconf/<version>` on macOS, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\gpconf\Cache\<version>` on Windows. The runner takes both roots and reads provider files from the data root and everything else from the corpus root; the gate reads a live capture's metadata from the data root. `gpconf/fetch.py` is `tools/fetch.py` moved into the package with its one root split in two (the fetch list and manifest from the corpus root, the files to and from the data root) and `--root` and `--data` options; its request logic is unchanged. `tools/fetch.py` is now a shim that runs the same code in a clone and, when imported, replaces itself in `sys.modules` with the package module, so the tests' `import fetch` and any patch reach the same code. `gpconf fetch` hands its arguments to that module, so it takes exactly the options `tools/fetch.py` always took. `gpconf run` prints `provider data: <folder> (<why>)` above the case table when the data root is not the corpus root, so a clone's output is unchanged; a missing corpus exits 2 with a reason; `check-tle` runs before any corpus lookup, since it checks the user's own file. D-148's fetch command now names the clone's `tools/fetch.py` where one exists and `python3 -m gpconf fetch` elsewhere, adding `--data DIR` when the data folder came from that flag and `--root DIR` for a bare corpus copy, and nothing for `GPCONF_DATA`, which reaches the fetch by itself. The fetch's dry-run summary, found while showing the installed copy, said "done: 0 request(s) made, 61 entries skipped" of the 61 requests it plans; it now says "dry run: no request made; a run would make 61 request(s) and leave 0 entries as they are". Alternatives: duplicating the fetch code in the package (rejected: two copies of the request logic under CelesTrak's policy is one too many); an environment switch to mark an installed copy (rejected: a clone must keep working with no setup, and the files beside the package already say which it is); one cache folder shared by all versions (rejected for now: a version's frozen values belong with the files it was built against; reusing matching files across versions is an open item in the plan note); printing the data folder on every run (rejected: it would change every clone's output for no information). Correction to D-148: its runner line and README sentence said a missing re-capture file "is fetched on a later run, once its original is at least two hours old". It is not: the fetch list marks the three re-capture entries as re-captures, and the fetch selects them only with `--include-recaptures`, whose help calls them maintainer-only (`tools/fetch.py` as committed at D-148, the selection filter and the flag's help text); a dry run of an installed copy plans 61 of the 64 entries. A normal fetch therefore always leaves `analyst-objects`, `bstar-and-derivative-forms` and `tle-omits-six-digit-objects` without their re-capture, and the runner's line and the README now say so; the fetch command below it is printed only when a file a normal fetch would bring is missing, since for a missing re-capture it would make no request. The error was the agent's, found while moving the fetch code. What moved: in the full-data runs of reference, naive and python-sgp4, every case, count, item and gate headline is identical to D-148's reports, compared field by field; `docs/FAILURES.md` is not regenerated. Scope, as instructed: no change to `pyproject.toml`, no build, no upload, no Action. The bundled corpus folder is assembled by the tests, which copy the shipped files into a scratch copy of the package, standing in for the build step of a later stage. Tests: `tests/test_install_layout.py` (new, 16): the cache folder on each platform, the precedence of the two roots, a clone printing no data line, and a copy outside the repository run in subprocesses with only that copy on `PYTHONPATH` and a scratch `HOME` (the copy, not the repository, is imported; an offline run finds the bundled corpus, names the cache and creates nothing; `list`; a fetch dry run plans every normal entry into the cache and writes nothing; `--data` and `GPCONF_DATA` redirect both run and fetch; with provider files copied into a data folder the copy runs every case; without a corpus, `run` exits 2 and `check-tle` still works), and `gpconf fetch` in-process with a stub where the request would be, writing to the data root and reading the list from the corpus root; `tools/fetch.py` and `gpconf.fetch` are the same module. No test makes a request. `tests/test_not_fetched.py`: the fetch command and the re-capture line updated. 178 tests pass in the private repository; a fresh public export (160 files, three new) runs 176 with 11 skipped for want of provider data, and CI's two offline commands exit 0 and 1 as before. README ("Fetching responsibly" and the not-fetched paragraph) and CHANGELOG updated.

**D-151 (2026-09-26) — v0.3.0 packaging, stage 2: the adapters move into the package and `--preset` arrives. Four presets, reference, naive, sgp4 and pyephem; each library preset records the version it was tested against and the report prints the version it found; naive is labelled a demonstration of failure. No packaging metadata, build, upload, Action or Node preset (owner instruction). No published count moved.** The three built-in adapters moved with `git mv` from `tests/adapters/` to `gpconf/adapters/` (`reference.py`, `naive.py`, `sgp4_adapter.py`), their code unchanged apart from docstrings and the python-sgp4 import message; the old files are shims that replace themselves in `sys.modules` with the package modules, so `--adapter tests.adapters.reference:Parser`, the tests, CI and the published documents that cite those names keep working and reach the same code. PyEphem's adapter came from the hand run's harness (`docs/handoff/batch-g/pyephem/adapter_pyephem.py`, corpus D-128, reported as pyephem#296) as `gpconf/adapters/pyephem_adapter.py`, its parsing unchanged; the harness's module-level list of refusals and its one stderr line per refused element set were for the findings note and are dropped, which changes no result; counted over a full run of the corpus, `readtle()` refuses no element set, so neither ever fired. It does not yet use the refusal channel (D-144), which the v0.4.0 plan gives every re-run; adopting it now would change its gate reading against the published row. `gpconf/presets.py` (new) names each preset's module, the library it exposes, the pip requirement, the optional extra and the tested version (python-sgp4 2.27, PyEphem 4.2.1). `gpconf run --preset NAME` loads it; `gpconf presets` lists the four with the installed version or the install command; the report's parser line reads, for example, "preset sgp4 (python-sgp4 2.27, the version it was tested with)", or names both versions and says results may differ from the published ones when they differ; the JSON report carries a `preset` block; a missing library is refused with exit status 2 and `pip install sgp4` or `pip install ephem`; `--preset` combined with another parser option is an error. The naive preset's line reads "preset naive (a demonstration of failure, not a parser anyone should use)". CI's two offline runs now use `--preset reference` and `--preset naive`. A conflict in the instruction, recorded rather than worked around: optional extras are declared in `pyproject.toml`, which this stage leaves alone; the registry records each extra (`sgp4`, `pyephem`) and its requirement, the install message names the library's own pip package, which is true today, and the extras go into `pyproject.toml` at the packaging stage. An export-allowlist gap, found while verifying the export and recorded for whoever adds the next folder: `PUBLIC_ALLOWLIST.txt` lists `gpconf/*.py`, a rule that was correct for as long as the package was one flat folder, and stopped being correct the moment this stage put `gpconf/adapters/` under it, because the allowlist's patterns do not recurse. Nothing failed: the private suite passed, since the private repository has every file, and the export dry run only reports a count. An export would have shipped the `tests/adapters/` shims without the modules they load, and public CI would have failed on the next push. It showed only as an export count five short of the files added (162 against 167). `gpconf/adapters/*.py` is added and the export now copies 167 files. So that the next folder cannot slip through the same way, the allowlist carries a comment above the package lines saying that patterns do not recurse and a new folder under `gpconf/` or `tests/` needs its own line, and `tests/test_export.py` now fails if any Python file under `gpconf/` or `tests/` is missing from an export. The check to make when adding a folder anywhere: does an existing allowlist pattern reach it? What moved in the published counts: nothing. The three built-in presets on full data in this repository give, for every case, count, item and gate, exactly the results of the D-148 reports behind `docs/FAILURES.md`, compared field by field; only the report's parser label differs, and `docs/FAILURES.md` is not regenerated. The PyEphem preset, run from a copy installed outside the repository (the corpus from a fresh public export, python-sgp4 2.27 and PyEphem 4.2.1 installed from PyPI into a scratch environment, the provider files of this repository as its data folder), fails the same six cases as the published hand-run row (PyEphem 4.2.1, 2026-09-24, 6 of 17); its other differences are all accounted for: D-143's empty-answer check turns three skipped cases into passes and adds a pass to `analyst-objects`; D-129 makes the SATCAT case not exercised; and the public export's scrub of SupGP values (D-049) drops the two Starlink G15-27 pairs from the pair lists of `mean-motion-derivative-convention` and `tle-vs-omm-precision-loss` (304 pairs to 302), so the installed copy never loads that CSV there and each case shows one skip fewer. The hand-run row stays as run. Alternatives: keeping the adapters in `tests/` (rejected: `tests/` is not part of an installed package); registering presets through package entry points (rejected for now: a standard-library table is enough for four, and entry points need the packaging metadata this stage leaves alone). Tests: `tests/test_presets.py` (new: the four presets and their descriptions, the naive label, the version label for a match and a mismatch, an unknown preset and a missing library refused with the install command, `--preset` not combinable, the old names being the package modules, a preset giving the adapter's results over the offline cases, the JSON block, the version printed by each installed library preset) a class in `tests/test_install_layout.py` running every preset from a copy outside the repository, and the export check in `tests/test_export.py`. 191 tests pass in this repository with one skip (PyEphem is not installed here); a fresh public export (167 files) runs 189 with 12 skipped; CI's two commands exit 0 and 1 as before. The export check fails when the `gpconf/adapters/` line is removed and passes with it.

**D-152 (2026-09-26) — The SatDump link stand-ins stay out of the MIT repository and its Zenodo archives, on the cautious reading of GPL-3.0; the public SatDump recipe will ship the harness and the list of symbol names the link needs, with the instruction to define each as an empty function or a default-constructed object; the harness's dead call to a predict function, and its redeclaration, are removed. Owner decision on the agent's analysis; analysis, not legal advice.** What the files are: `stubs.cpp` (20 lines, SatDump master f3d82ad) and `stubs-rel.cpp` (17 lines, release 1.2.2), kept with the hand run in `docs/handoff/batch-g/satdump/`, contain no line of SatDump. They include SatDump headers and define nine and seven SatDump symbols that SatDump's own compiled `tle.cpp` references and the harness never calls: the configuration object, the database pointer, a version string set to "harness", the member function `DBHandler::get_meta`, two HTTP helpers, the global logger and `Logger::logf`, an explicit specialisation of SatDump's `format_notated` template, and in the release file `main_cfg`, `saveUserConfig` and `eventBus`. Every body is empty or returns a constant. The permissive reading, which is genuinely strong: nothing expressive is copied, since the names and type signatures are dictated by the linker; names are not protected by copyright in the US under the Copyright Office's rule on words and short phrases; the Court of Justice of the EU held in SAS Institute v World Programming (2012) that a program's functionality and interfaces are not protected expression; the US Supreme Court held in Google v Oracle (2021) that reimplementing about 11,500 lines of declarations was fair use, and these are sixteen short definitions for non-commercial interoperability testing; GPL-3.0 counts as modifying only copying or adapting that needs copyright permission; the combined program is only built on the user's machine, and GPL-3.0 section 2 lets anyone make and run covered works they do not convey; MIT is GPL-compatible. On that reading the files could be published under MIT. The cautious reading: the stand-ins are not client code but replacements for parts of SatDump, defining SatDump's own objects and functions, including a member function of its `DBHandler` class and a specialisation of its template, and adapting part of a work is within GPL-3.0's definition of modifying; their selection, namespaces and types are dictated wholly by SatDump's internal structure at two commits; the US Federal Circuit held in 2014 that the structure and organisation of declarations can be protected, and the Supreme Court did not overturn that but decided fair use, a defence argued after an accusation. The deciding reasons: first, the asymmetry of the costs. Withholding the files costs a SatDump user the same sixteen empty definitions, written from the linker's errors. Publishing them wrongly would put GPL-derived material under MIT in a public repository, and every release of that repository is archived by Zenodo, where it cannot be withdrawn. Second, the corpus's policy to interpret licences strictly, never permissively (`CLAUDE.md`). The permissive reading is strong; it is not the standard the corpus sets. Consequences: the stand-in files stay private; the public SatDump recipe, in a later stage's harnesses folder, ships `harness.cpp` and `harness-rel.cpp`, which both readings accept as client code, since they call SatDump's functions the way any program uses a library and the FSF accepts GPL-compatible licences for such modules, together with the list of symbol names, which are not expression under either reading. The two harnesses redeclared `Julian_Date_of_Epoch` from SatDump's bundled predict fork and called it, but the result was discarded (`(void)jd`) and the ISO epoch was already built from a year computed locally with the fork's pivot, so the call and the redeclaration were dead code; both are removed, and the predict header stays because the harness calls `predict_parse_tle`. Verified by compiling the epoch helper on its own with clang, before and after: identical output over 63 year and day inputs. The full harness was not rebuilt, since no SatDump tree is on this machine. Open, not decided: publishing the stand-ins under GPL-3.0 in a separate repository, which would satisfy both readings; and asking SatDump's copyright holders, the one way to settle the question outright, which is the owner's contact to make.

**D-153 (2026-09-26) — The v0.3.0 plan's other five open items decided (owner decision on the agent's proposals).** (2) The Action, when a preset's library will not import, fails as a setup error with exit status 2 and a message saying that nothing ran and that this says nothing about the library. Results never fail a job, which is report-only with exit 0 for failed cases, but a run that tested nothing must not show green: that is the fault D-148 corrected in the runner, an unearned status. A library version other than the tested one is not a setup error; it runs and the report names both versions. The command line already exits 2 for a missing Python library (D-151), and its message gains the same two statements. Consequence for stage 3: a Node preset runs through the command protocol, one process per file, so a missing Node or library would otherwise show as a parse failure in every case, blamed on the library; stage 3 adds a preflight that checks both before any case runs. (3) tle.js: the preset `tle.js` reads the epoch from the raw year and day fields, reproducing the published row's 2 of 17, and `tle.js-api` reads it through `getEpochTimestamp()`, whose flooring to the millisecond (finding F3, a design choice) alone fails every TLE case in the hand run, 7 of 17. One harness serves both. (4) The `crosscheck` extra (python-sgp4, Skyfield, xmlschema, lxml) serves `tools/crosscheck.py` and `tools/validate_xml.py`, which the wheel does not carry; it is dropped from the published metadata, and maintainers install the pinned `tools/crosscheck-requirements.lock.txt`, to which the README's build paragraph will point. It was never published, so nothing breaks. It lands with the packaging metadata. (5) Fetched files are reused across corpus versions only in the stable tier and only on a SHA-256 match with the new version's manifest, copied with their metadata and marked as reused; live files are never reused, so an upgrade brings current data. Measured on the current fetch list: 22 of the 61 normal entries are stable, about 10 KB in all, and the other 39 are about 12.5 MB, so the saving is 22 requests per upgrade, a courtesy to CelesTrak rather than a gain in speed. Upstream drift is caught by the site's weekly check of the stable sources, not by a user's upgrade. It lands in the fetch code at a later stage. (6) The wheel is built by a staging script whose only input is a public export folder: it refuses a folder that holds any provider file or lacks `EXPORT-MANIFEST.txt`, walks the `gpconf` package recursively, copies the shipped corpus files into `gpconf/corpus/`, builds with plain setuptools package data and audits the wheel, every corpus file of which must match the export manifest's SHA-256. Rejected: hatchling's force-include, which maps whole folders, so a build run from the private tree would sweep in provider files, prevented only by procedure; and moving the data into `gpconf/` in the repository, which would break every published path, including the site's links and the Zenodo archives. Found while answering it: `pyproject.toml`'s `packages = ["gpconf"]` has omitted `gpconf/adapters` since D-151 and would have dropped it from a wheel, the allowlist fault of D-151 in another file; it is fixed with the staging script, at the packaging stage. The plan note records all six and the new open items.

**D-154 (2026-09-26) — v0.3.0 packaging, stage 3: the Node presets `satellite.js`, `tle.js` and `tle.js-api`, run from the working directory through `node --eval`, with a preflight that refuses a missing Node or library as a setup error, and `--module PATH`. No published result changed.** Resolution, tested before building on 2026-09-26 with Node 26.5.0 and satellite.js 7.1.0 and tle.js 5.0.3 installed in a scratch project: a harness file outside the user's project cannot import the library by bare name, even when run from inside the project (`ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`), which the plan had only inferred; `createRequire`, which the plan proposed, is the wrong tool, since satellite.js 7.1.0 exports only an `import` condition, which `require` resolution does not take; code passed to `node --input-type=module --eval` resolves bare imports from the working directory, like a script in the project, and `import.meta.resolve` there gives the entry file. So each preset runs its harness as `node --input-type=module --eval <source> -- <arguments>` with the working directory as its current directory, and `--module PATH` (the environment variable `GPCONF_NODE_MODULE` inside the harness) names the library's entry file for a checkout that cannot import itself by name. The harnesses moved into the package as `gpconf/adapters/satellitejs.mjs`, `tlejs.mjs` and `tlejs_vectors.mjs` from the hand runs (D-132, D-139); their parsing bodies are byte-identical to the hand runs' (compared with `diff`), and only how each finds its library and reads its arguments changed. `tle.js` passes `fields` and `tle.js-api` passes `api` to the one harness, and both use the vector-hook harness, as the hand run did (D-153). `gpconf/adapters/node_preflight.mjs` imports the library before any case runs and reports the Node version, the entry file and the library's version, read from the nearest `package.json` above the entry that names the library; a missing Node or a library the working directory cannot import is refused with exit 2 and a message naming the folder, the `npm install` command and `--module`, and saying that nothing ran and that this says nothing about the library (D-153). Without the preflight the command protocol would have turned a missing library into a parse failure in every case, charged to the library. The Python presets' refusal gained the same two statements. `CommandParser` takes an argument list besides a shell string: a list runs without a shell, in a given working directory with a given environment, and only an element that is exactly `{fmt}` is substituted, so the harness source passed as an argument is never altered. `gpconf presets` shows, for each Node preset, whether the library imports from the working directory, with its version and Node's; the JSON report's `preset` block adds the runtime. `PUBLIC_ALLOWLIST.txt` gains `gpconf/adapters/*.mjs`, the check D-151 asked for when adding files, here a new file type in an existing folder; `tests/test_export.py` now checks every file of the package, not only Python files. Results, from a copy installed outside the repository (the package from a fresh public export) and run from the scratch project with this repository's provider files as its data: each preset fails exactly the cases of its published row, 7 of 17 for satellite.js, 2 of 17 for tle.js and 7 for the API mode; every other difference from the hand runs is six extra passes, the D-143 empty-answer check on the six recorded empty answers, which the hand runs predate. The three built-in presets' full-data results are identical to the D-148 reports behind `docs/FAILURES.md`, compared field by field. Tests: `tests/test_node_presets.py` (new, 10): the registry, `--module` only with a Node preset, argument lists with `{fmt}`, the working directory and exit 3, a shell string still working, Node missing, a folder without the library for all three presets and from the command line (exit 2, no case run), the listing's missing-library line, and, where `GPCONF_TEST_NODE_PROJECT` names a project with the two libraries, each preset's version and offline statuses, fields and API differing only in the epoch, and `--module` from another folder. 201 tests pass in this repository with four skipped (PyEphem not installed; the three library tests without the project variable), and all ten Node tests pass with it; a fresh public export (172 files) runs 199 with 15 skipped; CI's two commands exit 0 and 1 as before.

**D-155 (2026-09-26) — v0.3.0 packaging, stage 4: `harnesses/`, the recipes for the five hand-run libraries that cannot be presets, in the public repository; best-effort, not in the pip package; SatDump's recipe as D-152 set it.** One folder per library, libsgp4, Gpredict, SatDump, astroz and gods-eye-view, holds the corpus's harness files, copied from the hand runs (`docs/handoff/batch-g/`) byte for byte except one comment line in astroz's `common.zig` that said whose code it is, and a README naming: the commit it was tested at, in full (read from the GitHub API on 2026-09-26: libsgp4 master 661e057a and PR #42 head 2b8d1413, Gpredict v2.6 c9fa018f, SatDump master f3d82adb and 1.2.2 7aef0fe8, astroz main d558933e, gods-eye-view main ce671ce5); the project's licence (Apache-2.0, GPL-2.0, GPL-3.0, GPL-3.0, MIT; the last from the application's `LICENSE` file, since GitHub's detector reports it as unrecognised); what the harness compiles against, internal source files or module paths in four of the five and the library's own classes in libsgp4, and that a recipe can break silently when the project moves; the toolchain as run; the published hand-run result; the build commands; and the run command, from a corpus clone's root or anywhere `gpconf` is installed. The folder's README says the recipes are not part of the `gpconf` package, are built by nothing in CI, were built and run once, and that the runner's later checks, such as D-143's, can change their pass and skip counts. The build commands are the ones recorded when the harnesses were built on 2026-09-24, with only paths changed, and two stated differences: the SatDump release links its own tree's predict files, where the run used objects compiled from master's tree [untested]; and the SatDump harnesses lost D-152's dead predict call. None of the five was rebuilt for this stage: the projects' sources are no longer on this machine. SatDump's folder holds `harness.cpp` and `harness-rel.cpp` and no stand-in: its README lists the nine symbols master's link needs and the seven 1.2.2's needs, each with its kind, and says to define each as an empty function or a default-constructed object; it says why there are no stand-ins in one sentence, that they define SatDump's own functions and objects and the corpus keeps anything written to replace part of a GPL-licensed program out of its MIT-licensed repository, without arguing the licence question. A private-only test checks that the list names exactly the symbols the corpus's own stand-ins defined, and a public test that no recipe source defines any of them. Two recipes keep a stand-in, and the owner confirmed that judgement on 2026-09-26, with this reasoning: a stand-in for a third-party library the project calls is not the same act as a stripped replacement for part of the project itself, which is what D-152 kept out. Gpredict's folder keeps `stubs/glib.h`, one macro mapping GLib's `g_ascii_strtod` onto the C library's `strtod`, the same conversion in the C locale the harness never leaves, and an empty `stubs/glib/gprintf.h`; GLib is licensed LGPL-2.1-or-later, and Gpredict itself contributes nothing to the stand-in. gods-eye-view's folder keeps `cesium-stub.mjs`, which binds the seven Cesium names the layer's update path touches to one inert placeholder, loaded through `cesium-hook.mjs` and `register.mjs`; Cesium is licensed Apache-2.0. A `harnesses/.gitignore` keeps what the recipes create inside a clone, including any SatDump stand-ins a user writes, from being committed. `PUBLIC_ALLOWLIST.txt` gains `harnesses/**/*`, which recurses, unlike the single-level package patterns, and `harnesses/.gitignore`, since glob skips dotfiles; `tests/test_export.py` checks every file under `harnesses/`, and fails with either line removed (checked). The README's contents table names the folder. Tests: `tests/test_harnesses.py` (new): the five folders and their exact files, each README's commits, licence and limits, the folder README, the SatDump list and its reason, no stand-in definitions, and, privately, the list against the stand-ins used and the copies against the hand runs' files. 209 tests pass in this repository with four skipped; a fresh public export (195 files) runs 207 with 17 skipped.

**D-156 (2026-09-26) — v0.3.0 packaging, stage 5: the staging script and the packaging metadata; the sdist and the wheel built locally from a fresh public export and audited; nothing registered, uploaded or published (owner instruction).** `tools/stage_package.py` (new, standard library only), as D-153 set it. Its only input is a public export folder, and it refuses one that lacks `EXPORT-MANIFEST.txt`, holds any provider file under `fixtures/<case>/raw/`, or holds `docs/handoff/`, which only the private repository has; that third check is an addition to D-153's two, since a private tree would otherwise pass them. It stages `pyproject.toml`, `README.md` and `LICENSE`; the whole `gpconf` package, walked recursively, so a new subfolder cannot be missed the way D-151 and D-153 found two patterns missing one; and the shipped corpus, `manifest.json`, `tools/fetchlist.json`, every case's `expected.json` and `case.md`, `derived/` and `vectors/`, into `gpconf/corpus/`, where `gpconf/locate.py` looks for it (D-150). Every staged file must match the export manifest's SHA-256, so an export changed after it was made is refused. With `--build` it runs the `build` frontend without isolation, which makes the sdist and builds the wheel from it, and audits both: every file under `gpconf/` must match the manifest, a corpus file under `gpconf/corpus/X` against the entry for `X`, and the audit fails on a mismatch, a file the manifest does not list, a provider file, any member other than the package and the build's own metadata, or a staged file missing from the archive. `pyproject.toml`: packages are found (`include = ["gpconf", "gpconf.*"]`) instead of listed, so `gpconf.adapters` is in, and so is any later subpackage, the structural answer to the explicit list D-153 found wrong; package data `corpus/**/*` for `gpconf` and `*.mjs` for `gpconf.adapters`; the extras `sgp4` (`sgp4`) and `pyephem` (`ephem`), unpinned, since a preset reports the version it finds against the one it was tested with; the `crosscheck` extra dropped, and the README's build paragraph now names `tools/crosscheck-requirements.lock.txt`; runtime dependencies still none. The licence moves to the current declaration, `license = "MIT"` with `license-files`, which needs setuptools 77 or later to build; the older table form is deprecated there. The version stays 0.2.1: the bump belongs to the release step. The build, from a fresh public export (196 files) with build 1.6.1 and setuptools 84.0.0 installed into a scratch environment used for nothing else: the sdist `gpconf-0.2.1.tar.gz`, 90 files, 4,516,949 bytes unpacked, 499,591 bytes as built; the wheel `gpconf-0.2.1-py3-none-any.whl`, 85 files, 4,442,875 bytes unpacked, 543,380 bytes as built; the two hold the same 79 package and corpus files, the sdist adding eleven build files and the wheel six metadata files; the audit found no problem in either, 60 corpus files and 19 package files each matching the export manifest; the build printed no warning; a second build gave the same wheel file list. Installed from the wheel into another scratch environment, with no network and no dependencies, the `gpconf` command ran `--preset reference` from an unrelated folder (4 pass, 13 need fetched data, the per-user cache named, nothing created in it) and refused `--preset sgp4` as a setup error. Not done, as instructed: no name registered on PyPI, no upload to TestPyPI or PyPI, no publish workflow. The README, which is also the package's long description, still has relative links that will not resolve on PyPI; that belongs to the documentation step. Tests: `tests/test_stage_package.py` (new, 8): the staged tree against the export, each refusal, the command line's exit 2, the audit passing a faithful wheel and sdist made here, the audit catching a changed file, a missing file, a provider file, an unlisted file and an unexpected member, and the metadata. 217 tests pass in this repository with four skipped; a fresh public export (197 files) runs 215 with 17 skipped.

**D-157 (2026-09-26) — v0.3.0 packaging, stage 6: stable-tier files are reused across corpus versions on a hash match, as D-153 set it; live files are never reused; the fetch and the run report say which files were reused rather than fetched.** Where: only in the per-user cache, where each corpus version has its own folder (D-150); a data folder named by `--data` or `GPCONF_DATA`, or a clone, holds one set of files and is not searched. What: when `gpconf fetch` fills a version's folder, `gpconf/fetch.py` looks in the other version folders beside it, newest first, for each stable-tier entry the folder lacks, and reuses a copy only when its bytes hash to the SHA-256 this version's manifest records and its metadata is present; the file and its `.meta.json` are copied, the metadata keeping the original `retrieved_at`, so the two-hour rule and the provenance still describe the one request that was made, and gaining `reused_from` (the version and the source path) and `reused_at`. A stable file whose bytes differ, or one without metadata, is requested; a live file is requested even when its bytes match, so that an upgrade brings current data. How it reads: a dry run prints `REUSE <url> (stable tier: the copy in corpus <version>'s cache holds the bytes this version's manifest records; copied, not requested)` and sums up "a run would make N request(s), reuse M file(s) from an earlier corpus version's cache"; a run prints `reused from <version>` against each file and "done: N request(s) made, M file(s) reused from an earlier corpus version's cache"; the runner reads `reused_from` from each provider file's metadata, lists the reused files per case in the JSON report under `reused`, and prints below the count line "N provider file(s) were reused from corpus <version>'s cache rather than fetched: stable tier, bytes matching this version's recorded SHA-256". Measured on the current fetch list, as D-153 expected: of the 61 normal entries, 22 are stable and 39 live. Shown from a copy installed outside the repository with a scratch home, whose per-user cache held an earlier version's folder, `0.2.0`, filled with this repository's 61 fetched files: the dry run planned 22 reuses and 39 requests and wrote nothing; a real run with a stand-in in place of the request function, which copied the file this repository already holds so that no request was made, reused the 22 stable files, called the stand-in 39 times, and its drift check found all 61 files matching the tested snapshot; the run report then said that 22 provider files were reused from corpus 0.2.0's cache. Tests: `tests/test_fetch_reuse.py` (new, 7): the version folders found newest first and non-version folders ignored; a stable file with matching bytes and metadata reused, a live file with matching bytes requested, a stable file with other bytes requested, one without metadata requested; the dry run's lines and summary with no request and no folder created; a run copying and marking the reused file, keeping `retrieved_at`, and caching it on the next run; reuse not attempted outside a per-user cache; and, where this repository's provider files exist, 22 reused and 39 requested over the real fetch list and the run report naming the reused files.

**D-158 (2026-09-26) — v0.3.0 packaging, stage 7: the GitHub Action, `action.yml` with `tools/action_report.py`, built as D-153 set it; it runs the corpus from its own checkout at the tag a workflow names, not from PyPI; tested locally and then live on the owner's scratch repository, seven jobs on macOS, Ubuntu and Windows, all green, with the three platforms' summaries character-identical; the live test found one fault, fixed here: the runner's advice to fetch into the Action's data folder, which is thrown away with the job.** The owner's question first: the plan had the Action install gpconf from PyPI, which does not exist yet; can it be built and tested now, or should it wait for the package? It can be built now, and without PyPI at all. A composite action runs from GitHub's download of the whole repository at the tag the workflow names, found in `github.action_path`, and that checkout already holds the runner and the corpus at exactly that tag. So the Action runs `gpconf` from it: nothing is installed, the tag pins code and corpus together, no step needs a package index, and there is nothing to swap in at release. The plan's `pip install gpconf==<version>` is replaced (plan note, section 6). Rejected: waiting for PyPI (it tests nothing more, and adds a dependency and a version to keep in step with the tag); installing the built wheel inside the Action (it would need the wheel published somewhere the Action can reach). `action.yml` is a composite action with the inputs `preset` (required), `python` (default `python3`, the interpreter the maintainer's library is installed in), `module` (Node presets) and `working-directory` (where a Node preset resolves its library), and the outputs `report`, `failed` and `exercised`. Its one step runs `tools/action_report.py` with the inputs in the environment, never in the shell text, so an input cannot inject a command. The script runs `gpconf run --preset <name>` against the Action's checkout with an empty data folder and `GPCONF_DATA` removed, so it is offline whatever the job sets. Failed cases exit 0: a result never becomes a verdict in someone else's CI. A preset that cannot run exits 2, and the summary says "Setup error: nothing was run.", gives the runner's message, which ends "Nothing was run; this says nothing about <library>.", and adds that this is a problem with the job's setup, not a result about the library. Any other runner failure is passed on and summarised as not a result. The job summary carries the parser line with the library version found and tested, how many of the seventeen cases were exercised offline and how many need provider data, which the Action does not fetch, the case table, the gate line, and the site's sentence "A count is a result against that version on that date, not a verdict on the project.", with the site's address. No badge. Local test, the composite step's own command run by bash with GitHub's environment and a fresh public export as the action's folder: python-sgp4 2.27 exit 0 with 2 failed and 3 exercised; naive exit 0 with 4 failed; pyephem without PyEphem exit 2 with the setup message; tle.js from a Node project exit 0 with 2 failed; reference with `GPCONF_DATA` pointing at this repository's fetched files still 4 exercised and 13 needing provider data. Live test, 2026-09-26, as the owner instructed, on a scratch repository of the owner's that is none of the three in use: `hneogy/gpconf-action-test`, private, created empty in the browser pane on the owner's word after they had seen the form. It holds a public export of this working tree without the corpus's own CI workflow, and the workflow in `docs/handoff/action-scratch-test/`, whose jobs call the Action through the scratch repository's tag `gpconf-action-test-1`, as a maintainer would, so the tag pin is tested too. The tag was pushed before `main` and the workflow runs on `main` only, so there was one run (36249122884). A seventh job, `reference-ubuntu`, was added at the owner's word before the first push, so that one preset runs on all three platforms and macOS can be compared with Ubuntu case for case. All seven jobs passed; each Action step's exit code, read from its raw log: python-sgp4 (2.27, installed by the job) 0, with 2 failed and 3 exercised; naive 0, with 4 failed; pyephem without PyEphem 2, the summary opening "Setup error: nothing was run." and the job green only because it expects that failure; tle.js 5.0.3 in a Node 22 project 0, with 2 failed; reference on macos-26-arm64, ubuntu-24.04 and windows-2025-vs2026 0 each, with 4 passing and 13 needing provider data. The three reference summaries are character-identical, compared by a script over the run page rather than by eye: the seventeen case rows, the count line and the gate line. Windows, not run before: the composite step ran under Git Bash (`C:\Program Files\Git\bin\bash.EXE`) with the image's `python`, and the Action's folder as a Windows path (`D:\a\_actions\...`) worked in the shell text; the step took 2.9 s, against 0.7 s on macOS and 0.6 s on Ubuntu. The fault the live test found: the runner's plain output ended with its usual advice, "Provider data is not shipped with the corpus; fetch it with" a command naming `tools/fetch.py` in the Action's folder and `--data` with the Action's empty data folder, and every missing item in the JSON report, which is the Action's `report` output, carried the same command. In the Action that advice is wrong: the Action never fetches, and the folder is thrown away with the job; on Windows the command also named `python3` and quoted a Windows path the Unix way. It never reached the job summary. The fix: `gpconf run --no-fetch-hint` (new, in `--help`), `Runner(fetch_hints=False)` underneath, leaves the fetch command out of the closing line, out of each missing item, which now reads "not on disk: provider data is not shipped with the corpus", and out of the drift message, and changes nothing else; `tools/action_report.py` passes it. Rejected: an environment variable, which the command line in the log would not show; and leaving the log as it was because the summary was right, since the JSON report is an output a maintainer's later steps may read. Also seen, not acted on: the tle.js step took 10 s against under 1 s for the Python presets [inferred: Node starting once per call; not examined]; and every Ubuntu job carried GitHub's notice that `ubuntu-latest` moves to Ubuntu 26 from 2026-10-19, the open item already listed. The scratch repository and its run are kept (owner: delete nothing). `PUBLIC_ALLOWLIST.txt` gains `action.yml`; `tests/test_export.py` checks that it and its script are exported. README: a CI paragraph after the presets. Tests: `tests/test_action.py` (new, 12): the composite definition, inputs only through the environment, no fetch and no install, the outputs and no badge, and the script's behaviour for a passing preset, failed cases, an unknown preset and a missing library, a Node preset without its library, and offline whatever `GPCONF_DATA` says; and, for the fix, no fetch command in the Action's log, summary or JSON report with every status unchanged, the command still named by a run without the flag with the same case table, and the drift message a sentence either way. 237 tests pass in this repository: seven skipped under the system python3 (three need `GPCONF_TEST_NODE_PROJECT`, four need python-sgp4 or PyEphem), one under the repository's `.venv` with the scratch Node project set (PyEphem, not installed there); a fresh public export (201 files) runs 235 under the system python3 with 21 skipped, every Action test among those run.

**D-159 (2026-09-26) — v0.3.0 packaging, stage 8: the README, the changelog and the version constants, and the README's relative links for the PyPI page, which are made absolute in the copy the package carries and left relative in the repository; no version bump, which belongs to the release; nothing uploaded, PyPI untouched.** Links first, counted before anything changed: the README holds 13 occurrences of `](`: eleven absolute links (six to GitHub issues and pull requests, four DOIs, and the Zenodo badge's link), the badge's image, also absolute, and one relative link, `` [`AUDIT.md`](AUDIT.md) ``; no reference-style link, no HTML, no in-page anchor. The packaging plan had expected many relative links, from reading rather than counting; the count found one. About thirty repository paths are written as code (`docs/FAILURES.md`, `DECISIONS.md` and the like); they are links neither on GitHub nor on PyPI and were not changed. The fix keeps both places working. `tools/stage_package.py` stages README.md as the PyPI description, with every relative link, image, reference definition and HTML `href` or `src` made absolute and pinned to the tag of the version being built: `https://github.com/hneogy/gp-omm-conformance/blob/v<version>/<path>`, `tree/` for a folder, `raw.githubusercontent.com` for an image, an in-page anchor pointed at the README on GitHub [inferred: PyPI is not relied on to keep heading ids]. So the PyPI page links to the files of the version a user installed, while the repository's README keeps its relative link, which resolves on GitHub, in a clone, in a fork and at any tag. Code spans and fenced blocks are left alone. A relative link to a path the export does not hold, or outside the repository, is refused, since the page would carry a dead link. The audit now checks README.md in the sdist against the staged copy, and fails a build whose long description (PKG-INFO, or the wheel's METADATA, what PyPI shows) holds a relative link. Rejected: rewriting the repository's links to absolute URLs, which would send every reader of a clone, a fork or an older tag to main on GitHub; a separate long-description file, a second README to keep in step; pinning to main rather than the tag, which would drift from the version installed. Checked by a local build of this working tree's export, with build 1.6.1 and setuptools 84.0.0 in the scratch environment of D-156 and nothing uploaded: wheel 85 files, sdist 90, the same as D-156, the audit finding no problem in either; the wheel's METADATA and the sdist's PKG-INFO carry the staged README exactly as their description, as `text/markdown`, with the one link at `.../blob/v0.2.1/AUDIT.md`, which a build at the release gives as `v0.3.0`, and no relative link; the wheel's METADATA carries the four `Project-URL` lines below. Version constants, counted over every tracked file with `git grep -F 0.2.1` (23 lines in 12 files outside the private handoff folder) rather than taken from the plan's hand count of five places: set by hand in `gpconf/__init__.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `tools/make_manifest.py`'s `CORPUS_VERSION` and `CITATION.cff` (with its release date); stated in the README's status paragraph, citation sentence and version-DOI sentence; generated in `manifest.json`, `MANIFEST.md` and `docs/FAILURES.md`; the rest read at run time, history (the changelog's headings, six decision entries, the harness note on the runner of 2026-09-24, the earlier DOIs) or test data. Nothing is bumped: the release does that. `tests/test_versions.py` (new, 3) fails unless the four hand-set constants, the two generated copies and the README's two version statements carry one version, the citation date agrees in both places, and the versioning rule reads the same in the README and the changelog; a bump that misses one cannot pass. Decision 1 of 2026-09-25 is applied: the rule's Minor line, in the README and in the changelog's preamble, reads "refreshed live snapshots, added cases, or an additive protocol change". README: the quick start gains the pip path beside the clone (`pip install gpconf`; before any fetch, the four cases whose files ship in the package run and thirteen say they need provider data, as the installed wheel of D-156 showed; `gpconf fetch`; then all seventeen), a line saying the command is `gpconf` installed and `python3 -m gpconf` in a clone, the `sgp4` and `pyephem` extras beside the plain installs, a paragraph on what the pip package carries and what stays in the repository, `gpconf fetch` beside `tools/fetch.py` in the contents table, and, under Versioning, that the package carries the version of the corpus it ships. The changelog's Unreleased section is rewritten for someone installing gpconf for the first time rather than someone following this log: installing and running, testing a library without writing an adapter, CI, and the report, in terms of commands and output, each entry keeping its decision numbers; the earlier items D-125, D-129 and D-142 to D-148 stay, rewritten the same way; details that matter only inside the build (the command parser's argument lists, the staging script's internals, the re-capture wording fix) are left to this log. At the owner's word, `pyproject.toml` gains a `[project.urls]` table, the only navigation a PyPI visitor gets: Homepage, the site (https://gpconf.neogy.dev); Repository; Issues, the repository's tracker, which is enabled; and DOI, the Zenodo concept DOI, which resolves to the latest release (each answered HTTP 200 on 2026-09-26). The code-written paths stay code, by the owner's decision: they read as paths, not links, and turning some thirty of them into links would clutter the README on GitHub to fix a problem nobody has on PyPI. The release order this stage creates is written as ordered steps in the private release checklist (`docs/handoff/release-checklist-v0.3.0.md`, new; the plan note had referred to a checklist that did not exist): the tag `v0.3.0` is pushed before the package is built and uploaded, and the links are checked to resolve between the two, since the description points at `blob/v0.3.0/` and a PyPI description cannot be changed without a new release. That inverts the usual order of upload, confirm, then tag, so it is a numbered step, not a note. Tests: `tests/test_stage_package.py` gains six (14 in all): every relative form rewritten at the tag, absolute links and code left alone, a dead or outside link refused, the staged README against the repository's, the audit reading the long description, and the four sidebar links. 246 tests pass in this repository with seven skipped under the system python3; a fresh public export (202 files) runs 244 with 21 skipped.

**D-160 (2026-09-26) — SECURITY.md for the public repository (owner instruction): where to report a vulnerability, what is in scope, and that the package has no runtime dependencies, in the shortest honest form rather than a template.** Checked first: the repository has no CONTRIBUTING and no SECURITY file, and `.github/` holds only the CI workflow; the README and the changelog say "standard library" in passing, and the README says what makes a useful report of a wrong value, but neither names a reporting channel for vulnerabilities or a scope, so the new file repeats nothing. Reports go to GitHub's private vulnerability reporting, at the repository's `security/advisories/new` address, with a request not to open a public issue; the setting was confirmed enabled through GitHub's API on 2026-09-26 (`{"enabled": true}`), and the address answers, sending a signed-out visitor to sign in first. In scope, as the owner set it: the runner (`gpconf/`), the adapters behind its presets, the fetch code (`gpconf/fetch.py`, `tools/fetch.py`), the staging script (`tools/stage_package.py`) and the Action (`action.yml`, `tools/action_report.py`). Not in scope: the corpus data, the expected values, derived files and vectors frozen from public provider responses and published standards, where a wrong value is an ordinary issue; and `harnesses/`, best-effort recipes that compile against other projects' internals (D-155). The wording is exact about what the repository holds: expected values, derived files and vectors, not the provider responses themselves, which each user fetches (D-019). The no-runtime-dependencies sentence rests on `pyproject.toml`'s `dependencies = []`; the file names the two optional extras and says that the Node presets use the Node and the library the user installed. Left out, since the maintainer has not decided them: a supported-versions table, a response time, any bounty. `PUBLIC_ALLOWLIST.txt` gains `SECURITY.md`: the allowlist names root files one by one, so without the line the export would have left the file out; a fresh export carries it byte for byte and lists it in `EXPORT-MANIFEST.txt`. It stays out of the pip package, whose staging takes only README.md, LICENSE and pyproject.toml from the top level (D-156). Tests: `tests/test_export.py` checks that the file is exported; `tests/test_security.py` (new, 4) checks that every path it names exists, so a move cannot leave it pointing at nothing, that reports go to the private reporting address, and that the sentence on dependencies holds only while `pyproject.toml` declares none. Added to the scope after the first draft: `tools/verify_against_spacetrack.py`, public, which asks a user for their Space-Track username and password. The owner's list had left it out; the omission was flagged when the draft was shown, and the owner added it as the place a vulnerability would matter most. `tests/test_security.py` holds it in the in-scope paragraph. 251 tests pass in this repository with seven skipped under the system python3; a fresh public export (204 files) runs 249 with 21 skipped.

**D-161 (2026-09-26) — `docs/ADAPTERS.md`, the adapter-writing guide (owner instruction), written for a maintainer with twenty minutes who knows their library and nothing about the corpus; every claim about the protocol checked against the runner's code rather than the README, every code example copied from a shipped adapter, and the README's adapter protocol moved into it.** Contents, in the owner's order: the two ways to write an adapter (a Python class the runner imports, a command for a parser in any other language) and when each is right; the Python protocol (what the runner calls, what each field must be, what an unexpected return does), worked through PyEphem's adapter as the complete minimal one and python-sgp4's as the real one; the command protocol (the JSON in and out, exit codes 0, 3 and other, the 120-second limit, floats against strings), worked through `satellitejs.mjs`, with `tlejs_vectors.mjs` for `--vectors-cmd` and the `--write-cmd` contract; the refusal channel (D-144); how returned records are counted as loaded, misidentified, refused and dropped; the vector hooks and the writer case; fetching, running, reading the report and `--json`; and what an adapter must not do. Method: the protocol was read from `gpconf/runner.py` (the parse call and its normalisation, `split_refusals`, the values counts, `run_vectors`, `run_writer`, `CommandParser`), `gpconf/gates.py`, `gpconf/presets.py` and `gpconf/__main__.py`; the behaviour the guide states for unexpected output was confirmed with probe adapters in a scratch folder, outside the repository (a non-numeric decimal, an unreadable epoch or a dict instead of a list fails the whole file as `parse`; a string, fractional or zero catalog number, a missing core field, an empty return, `Unsupported` and unknown keys each report as the guide says); and every command the guide shows was run as printed (python-sgp4 2.27 through `--adapter`: 17 cases, 7 failing, as published; the satellite.js preset and the same harness through an explicit `--cmd`: identical tables; tle.js with `--vectors-cmd`; the reference adapter), except `python3 tools/fetch.py`, run only as `--dry-run`, which makes no request, and the two `pip install` lines. The six excerpts are inserted from the files by line range (`pyephem_adapter.py` 9 to 55, the whole file below its docstring, `sgp4_adapter.py` 4 to 66 and 68 to 78, `satellitejs.mjs` 8 to 77, `tlejs_vectors.mjs` 8 to 34, `reference.py` 39 to 75), one line is quoted from `sgp4_adapter.py` (45), and the sample report is a real run's output. The PyEphem example was not run in this session, since PyEphem is not installed here; its last recorded run is D-151 [untested since]. Where the code and the documents disagreed, the guide follows the code: the runner's module docstring listed four vector hooks where the code calls five (`parse_catalog_id` was missing) and `CommandParser`'s promised a `{path}` placeholder the code never substitutes, and both docstrings are corrected; the README said the refusal declaration goes first in the list, where the code takes it anywhere; with `--vectors-cmd` the runner asks the command for all five hooks, so an operation it answers with an error fails its valid vectors instead of skipping (the owner's brief said an adapter without hooks skips; that holds for a Python class and for a command without `--vectors-cmd`, not per operation); a record returned with the wrong catalog number counts both as misidentified and as dropped; and loaded means the identity only, not correct values. README overlap, so that it is not restored: moved into the guide, the "Writing an adapter" protocol sketch (placeholder code, `my_library` and the like, that could not run), the refusal-channel paragraph and its snippet, the paragraph on values, hooks and `write_tle` (its account of how written lines are judged now sits under the guide's vector hooks), and the Quick start's three paragraphs on `--adapter`, `--cmd` and `--write-cmd`; stayed in the README, a four-sentence "Writing an adapter" that links to the guide, a one-paragraph pointer in the Quick start, the contents table's `docs/` row naming the guide, and the sections on how any parser's results are judged, which the guide links rather than repeats: Tolerances (the runner's own output tells readers to see "README, Tolerances"), the paragraphs on `not-fetched`, unreadable files and drift, the provider's empty answer and the gate. `tests/test_adapters_doc.py` (new, 5) fails if the README's adapter section grows a code block, passes 150 words or loses its link; it also holds every excerpt to its file at the lines named, the one-line quotation to its line, every path the guide names to an existing file, and every section it links, its own or the README's, to an existing heading. `PUBLIC_ALLOWLIST.txt` gains `docs/ADAPTERS.md`, since `docs/` files are named one by one, and `tests/test_export.py` checks it is exported. The README now has three relative links, `AUDIT.md` and the guide twice; the PyPI staging of D-159 makes all three absolute (a local build: "3 relative link(s) made absolute", no audit problem in the wheel or the sdist), `tests/test_stage_package.py` expects them, and the private release checklist's steps 9 and 10 name them. 257 tests pass in this repository with seven skipped under the system python3; a fresh public export (206 files) runs 255 with 21 skipped.

**D-162 (2026-09-26) — astroz follow-up, from the owner's account on the owner's word: the fixes for #97 and #98 re-run and confirmed on PR #99, and a new finding, the seconds in `days2mdhms` and `jday` passing through a half-precision float, filed as issue #102.** The maintainer merged PR #99 (description "fixes #98 & #97") into main as 4ca34f2 at 2026-09-26T20:00:14Z and closed both issues as completed. Re-run with the D-140 harness and vectors program built unchanged against 4ca34f2, and probes of my own, on Zig 0.16.0 with the Batch G runner: every Alpha-5 decode vector in the corpus passes (the 64 valid fields, X, Y and Z at both ends of each letter, and the six invalid ones refused); all 604 derived Alpha-5 element sets decode to the right catalog numbers; `Tle.epoch` is seconds past J2000 within 20 µs of the exact value on all 910 TLE element sets in the corpus, the 1998 record included; and `days2mdhms` puts the last day of every month on its own day, checked at four times of day on every day of 1998, 2000, 2024, 2026 and 2100. [tested] **The confirmation was posted on PR #99 2026-09-26T22:02:17Z as https://github.com/ATTron/astroz/pull/99#issuecomment-5850276993**, as paragraphs: drafted as two bullets, it rendered with each issue's full title in front of its bullet, since GitHub expands a reference that opens a list item, so the markers were removed and nothing else changed. The same checks found the new finding. `Datetime.seconds` is an `f16`, and both functions pass the seconds through it, `days2mdhms` by way of `fromYearDoy` and `jday` by way of `initDatetime`. Over 100,000 evenly spaced instants of one day, `days2mdhms` returns the second more than 1 ms off at 79,200 of them and by up to 15.5 ms (00:03:59.328 comes back as 59.3125), and near the end of a minute it returns 60.0 with the minute unchanged; the `fr` that `jday` returns is more than 1 ms off at 79,968, by up to 15.5 ms, and its `(jd, fr)` is what the `astroz.api` example hands to `sgp4`, so the error reaches propagation input, not only a displayed time. python-sgp4 2.27, whose API both functions follow, returns 59.328 and 33.6 for two of those instants where astroz returns 59.3125 and 33.59375, and 59.99 where astroz returns 60.0. [tested] PR #99 did not touch it, and it is not part of #98, which was about the day; under the D-134 convention it stayed out of this log until filed. The tracker was searched first (all 101 issues and pull requests and all 51 comments; nothing on it), and **the report was filed 2026-09-26T22:06:33Z as https://github.com/ATTron/astroz/issues/102**, prose only under GPL-3.0, both functions named in the title at the owner's instruction. After the checks the draft rested on and before the filing, the maintainer published release v0.13.0 (22e8ee0, 20:46:28Z; PyPI 0.13.0). Built from the tag, the harness's runs in both epoch modes and every probe give output identical to 4ca34f2's, so the release carries both fixes and the `f16` seconds, and #102's lines and numbers hold for it. The site's astroz row now says released for both issues and stays as run against d558933 (site S-051). Records, outside the export allowlist: `docs/handoff/batch-g/astroz/` (the findings updates, drafts C and D with their status lines, the probe sources, and the run and probe outputs for 4ca34f2 and v0.13.0). Alternatives: a comment on #98 for the seconds (rejected: #98 is closed and was about the day, and a separate cause deserves its own report); a report on `days2mdhms` alone, as first briefed (rejected on the owner's instruction once `jday` was found to share the field, since `jday` feeds propagation); a confirmation on each issue rather than on the pull request (rejected: PR #99 closed both, and one comment reaches the maintainer once).
